| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | E forse quello che ho detto<br><br><br>sembrerà meno crudele se riuscirò a farti capire<br>che in realtà parlavo di me stessa. |  
| EDWARD | [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] ha ragione. È da qui che si parte.<br><br><br>Se adesso scopri, Peter, cose su te stesso<br>che non ti va di guardare in faccia: [[motifs/well\|beh]], ricorda soltanto<br>che certi uomini devono imparare cose ben peggiori<br>su se stessi, e le imparano più tardi,<br>quando è più difficile riprendersi e ricominciare.<br>Per te non è così duro. Tu sei buono per natura. |  
| PETER | Scusatemi. Non credo di aver afferrato<br><br><br>tutto quello che avete detto. Ma vi sono grato lo stesso.<br>Sapete, per tutto il [[motifs/time\|tempo]] che avete parlato,<br>un solo pensiero mi girava e rigirava in testa —<br>che mi sono interessato soltanto a me stesso:<br>e questo non basta per [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Devi aver imparato a guardare la gente, Peter,<br><br><br>quando la guardi con l’occhio del cinema:<br>cioè quando non sei preso da te stesso<br>ma sei soltanto un occhio. Un giorno arriverai a pensare a [[characters/celia\|Celia]]<br>così. E allora la capirai<br>e sarai riconciliato, e felice nel pensiero di lei. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sir Henry, c’è una cosa che voglio dirle.<br><br><br>Mentre Alex ci raccontava che cosa era accaduto a [[characters/celia\|Celia]]<br>io guardavo il suo volto. E dall’espressione sembrava<br>che il modo in cui è morta non la turbasse,<br>né il fatto che sia morta perché non ha voluto abbandonare<br>pochi indigeni morenti. |  
| REILLY | Chi lo sa, signora Chamberlayne,<br><br><br>quale differenza abbia fatto per gli indigeni che morivano<br>o per lo stato d’animo in cui sono morti? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sono disposta a concederlo. Quello che mi ha colpita, però,<br><br><br>è che il suo volto non mostrava né sorpresa né orrore<br>per il modo in cui è morta. Non so se la conoscesse.<br>Sospetto di sì. In ogni caso sapeva di lei.<br><br><br>Eppure mi è parso che la sua espressione fosse di… soddisfazione! |  
| REILLY | Signora Chamberlayne, o io sono molto trasparente<br><br><br>oppure lei è molto perspicace. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, Henry!<br><br><br>[[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] è molto più osservatrice di quanto lei creda.<br>Credo che l’abbia messa con le spalle al muro. |  
| REILLY | Descrive la situazione con esattezza, [[characters/julia\|Julia]].<br><br><br>Le dispiace se cito dei versi, signora Chamberlayne? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh no, mi [[motifs/love\|piacerebbe]] molto sentirla recitare dei versi… |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Ha segnato un punto, Henry. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | …se rispondono alla mia domanda. |  
| REILLY | Prima che Babilonia fosse [[motifs/dust\|polvere]]<br>il mago Zoroastro, o mia morta [[archetypes/child\|creatura]],<br>incontrò la propria immagine che passeggiava nel [[motifs/garden\|giardino]].<br>Quell’apparizione, solo fra gli uomini, egli vide.<br>Ché sappi: vi sono due mondi, di vita e di [[motifs/death\|morte]]:<br>l’uno è quello che tu contempli; ma l’altro<br>sta sotto la [[motifs/grave\|tomba]], dove abitano<br>le ombre di tutte le forme che pensano e vivono<br>finché la [[motifs/death\|morte]] le unisca e più non si separino!<br>Quando incontrai per la prima volta la signorina Coplestone, in questa stanza,<br>vidi l’immagine, ritta dietro la sua sedia,<br>di una [[characters/celia-coplestone\|Celia Coplestone]] il cui volto mostrava lo stupore<br>dei primi cinque minuti dopo una [[motifs/death\|morte]] violenta.<br>Se questo mette a dura prova la sua credulità, signora Chamberlayne,<br>le chiedo soltanto di accogliere l’ipotesi<br>che un’intuizione improvvisa, in certe menti,<br>tenda a esprimersi subito in un’immagine.<br>A me capita, a volte. Era dunque evidente<br>che avevo davanti una donna condannata a [[motifs/death\|morte]].<br>Quello era il suo destino. L’unica domanda,<br>allora, era: che genere di [[motifs/death\|morte]]? Non potevo saperlo;<br><br><br>perché spettava a lei scegliere la via della vita<br>che conduce alla [[motifs/death\|morte]] e, senza conoscerne la fine,<br>scegliere pure la forma della [[motifs/death\|morte]]. Sappiamo quale [[motifs/death\|morte]] ha scelto.<br>Io non sapevo che sarebbe morta in questo modo;<br>lei non lo sapeva. Perciò tutto quello che potevo fare<br><br><br>era guidarla nella via della preparazione.<br>Quella via, che lei accettò, portava a questa [[motifs/death\|morte]].<br>E se questa non è una [[motifs/death\|morte]] felice, quale [[motifs/death\|morte]] lo è? |  
| EDWARD | Vuol dire che, avendo scelto questa forma di [[motifs/death\|morte]],<br><br><br>non ha sofferto come soffrono le persone comuni? |  
| REILLY | Non è affatto ciò che intendo. Semmai il contrario.<br><br><br>Direi che ha sofferto tutto quello che dovremmo soffrire<br>in [[concepts/fear\|paura]] e [[motifs/pain\|dolore]] e ribrezzo — tutto questo insieme —<br>e nella riluttanza del [[concepts/body\|corpo]] a diventare una cosa.<br>Direi che ha sofferto di più, perché più consapevole<br>di noi altri. Ha pagato il prezzo più alto<br>in [[concepts/suffering\|sofferenza]]. Anche questo fa parte del disegno. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Forse aveva attraversato un’agonia più grande, prima.<br><br><br>Voglio dire — non so nulla dei suoi ultimi due anni. |  
| REILLY | Questo mostra una certa penetrazione da parte sua, signora Chamberlayne;<br><br><br>ma un’esperienza simile si può soltanto adombrare<br>in miti e immagini. Per parlarne<br>ricorriamo alle tenebre, ai labirinti, ai terrori del Minotauro.<br>Ma quel mondo non prende il posto di questo.<br>Immagina forse che il Santo nel [[motifs/desert\|deserto]],<br>col male spirituale sempre alle spalle,<br>soffrisse meno di noi per la fame, l’umidità, le intemperie,<br>i disturbi intestinali e la [[concepts/fear\|paura]] dei leoni,<br>il freddo della [[motifs/night\|notte]] e la calura del giorno? |  
| EDWARD | Ma se questo era giusto — se era giusto per [[characters/celia\|Celia]] —<br><br><br>allora dev’esserci qualcos’altro di terribilmente sbagliato,<br>e noi altri siamo in qualche modo implicati in quello sbaglio.<br>Dovrei parlare solo per me. Sono certo di esserlo io. |  
| REILLY | Mi lasci liberare la sua mente da un ostacolo:<br><br><br>deve cercare di staccarsi da ciò che ancora sente<br>come una sua responsabilità. |  
| EDWARD | Non riesco a evitare la sensazione<br><br><br>che, in qualche modo, la mia responsabilità<br>sia maggiore di quella di un branco di selvaggi mezzi impazziti. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, Edward, lo sapevo! Sapevo che cosa stavi pensando!<br><br><br>Non ti aiuta sapere che anch’io mi sento colpevole? |  
| REILLY | Se fossimo tutti giudicati secondo le conseguenze<br><br><br>di ogni nostra parola e azione, al di là dell’intenzione<br>e al di là della nostra limitata comprensione<br>di noi stessi e degli altri, saremmo tutti condannati.<br>Signora Chamberlayne, spesso devo prendere una decisione<br>che può significare guarigione o rovina per un paziente —<br>e a volte ho preso la decisione sbagliata.<br>Quanto alla signorina Coplestone: poiché ritenete la sua [[motifs/death\|morte]] uno spreco<br>date la colpa a voi stessi, e poiché date la colpa a voi stessi<br>pensate che la sua vita sia stata sprecata. È stata trionfale.<br>Ma io non sono più responsabile del trionfo —<br>e sono responsabile della sua [[motifs/death\|morte]] tanto quanto voi. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Eppure so che continuerò a incolparmi<br><br><br>di essere stata così cattiva con lei… così maligna.<br>Continuerò a vederla nel momento<br>in cui ci disse addio, due anni fa. |  
| EDWARD | La tua responsabilità è nulla rispetto alla mia, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Non ne sono così sicura. Se avessi capito te,<br><br><br>forse non avrei frainteso [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |  
| REILLY | Dovrete convivere con questi ricordi e trasformarli<br><br><br>in qualcosa di nuovo. Solo accettando<br>il passato ne muterete il significato. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Henry, credo che sia [[motifs/time\|tempo]] che dica qualcosa anch’io:<br><br><br>ognuno fa una scelta, dell’uno o dell’altro genere,<br>e poi deve accettarne le conseguenze. [[characters/celia\|Celia]] scelse<br>una via la cui conseguenza fu Kinkanja.<br>Peter ha scelto una via che lo porta a Boltwell:<br>e là deve andare… |  
| PETER | Capisco che cosa vuoi dire.<br><br><br>Vorrei non doverlo fare. Ma la macchina starà aspettando,<br>e gli esperti — quasi me n’ero dimenticato.<br>Mi rendo conto che non posso tirarmene fuori —<br>e che altro potrei fare? |  
| ALEX | È il tuo film.<br><br><br>E so che Bela se ne aspetta grandi cose. |  
| PETER | Allora vado. |  
| EDWARD | Ti rivedremo, Peter,<br><br><br>prima che tu lasci l’Inghilterra? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Cerca di venire a trovarci.<br><br><br>Sai, credo che farebbe bene a tutti noi —<br>a te e a me e a Edward… parlare di [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |  
| PETER | Grazie mille. Ma non questa [[motifs/time\|volta]] —<br><br><br>proprio non riuscirò. |  
| EDWARD | Ma alla tua prossima visita? |  
| PETER | La prossima [[motifs/time\|volta]] che vengo in Inghilterra, ve lo prometto.<br><br><br>Ho davvero una gran voglia di rivedervi entrambi.<br>Addio, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]. Addio, Alex. Addio, Sir Henry.             [Esce] |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | …E ora la conseguenza della scelta dei Chamberlayne<br><br><br>è un cocktail party. Devono essere pronti ad affrontarlo.<br>I loro ospiti possono arrivare da un momento all’altro. |  
| REILLY | [[characters/julia\|Julia]], lei ha ragione. Ed è giusto anche<br><br><br>che i Chamberlayne diano ora un ricevimento. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | E io sto pensando, da questi ultimi cinque minuti,<br><br><br>a come potrò affrontare i miei ospiti. Vorrei che fosse già finito.<br>Voglio dire… sono contenta che siate venuti… sono contenta che Alex ce l’abbia detto…<br>e Peter doveva saperlo… |  
| EDWARD | Adesso credo di capire… |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Allora [[concepts/hope\|spero]] che me lo spiegherai! |  
| EDWARD | Oh, non è molto<br><br><br>quello che capisco finora! Ma Sir Henry ha detto,<br>mi pare, che ogni momento è un nuovo inizio;<br>e [[characters/julia\|Julia]], che vivere è soltanto continuare;<br>e in qualche modo le due idee sembrano combaciare. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Ma lo stesso… non ho voglia di vedere questa gente. |  
| REILLY | È il fardello che le è stato assegnato. E quanto al ricevimento,<br><br><br>sono certo che sarà un [[success\|successo]]. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | E io credo, Henry,<br><br><br>che dovremmo andarcene prima che il ricevimento cominci.<br>Se la caveranno meglio senza di noi. Anche tu, Alex. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Non vogliamo che ve ne andiate! |  
| ALEX | Abbiamo un altro impegno. |  
| REILLY | E in quell’occasione non sarò inatteso. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Su, Henry. Su, Alex. Andiamo dai Gunning. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono [[characters/julia\|JULIA]], REILLY e ALEX] |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Edward, come sto? |  
| EDWARD | Molto [[motifs/well\|bene]].<br><br><br>Direi quasi: mai stata meglio. Ma stai sempre al tuo meglio. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, Edward, così rovini tutto. Nessuna donna può credere<br><br><br>di essere sempre al suo meglio. Sei piuttosto trasparente,<br>sai, quando cerchi di tirarmi su.<br>Dire che sto sempre al meglio può solo voler dire il peggio. |  
| EDWARD | Non imparerò mai a fare un complimento. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Quello che avresti dovuto fare era ammirare il mio vestito. |  
| EDWARD | Ma ti ho già detto quanto mi piace. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Ma da allora sono successe tante cose. E poi,<br><br><br>a volte fa piacere sentirsi fare due volte lo stesso complimento. |  
| EDWARD | E adesso al ricevimento. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Adesso al ricevimento. |  
| EDWARD | Sarà presto finito. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Vorrei che cominciasse. |  
| EDWARD | Ecco il campanello. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, che gioia. È cominciato.<br><br><br>SIPARIO<br>La melodia di One-eyed Riley (pagina 137), trascritta sotto dettatura dell’autore dalla signorina Mary Trevelyan.<br><br><br>Gli interpreti della prima rappresentazione<br>al<br>Festival di Edimburgo,<br>22–27 agosto 1949<br><br><br>Regia di E. MARTIN BROWNE<br>Scene di ANTHONY HOLLAND<br>Produzione di SHEREK PLAYERS LTD.<br>in collaborazione con THE ARTS COUNCIL<br><br>IL SEGRETARIO PRIVATO<br>Personaggi<br>[[characters/sir-claude-mulhammer\|SIR CLAUDE MULHAMMER]]<br>EGGERSON<br>[[characters/colby-simpkins\|COLBY SIMPKINS]]<br>B. KAGHAN<br>LUCASTA ANGEL<br>LADY ELIZABETH MULHAMMER |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="The Cocktail Party — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/double,archetype/fool,motif/desire,motif/home,motif/leaves,motif/love,motif/machine,motif/memory,motif/night,motif/secrets,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,concept/ambition,concept/body,concept/courage,concept/doubt,concept/hope,concept/mercy,concept/solitude,concept/truth,concept/wonder,form/novel,setting/castle,setting/denmark,setting/london,setting/oxford,character/bottom,character/celia,character/celia_coplestone,character/edward_chamberlayne,character/julia,character/lavinia,character/nurse"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | The drawing-room of the Chamberlaynes’ [[settings/london\|London]] flat. Early evening. [[characters/edward-chamberlayne\|EDWARD CHAMBERLAYNE]], [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] SHUTTLETHWAITE, [[characters/celia-coplestone\|CELIA COPLESTONE]], PETER QUILPE, ALEXANDER MACCOLGIE GIBBS, and an UNIDENTIFIED GUEST. |
| ALEX | You’ve missed the point completely, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]:<br><br><br>There were no tigers. That was the point. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Then what were you doing, up in a tree:<br><br><br>You and the Maharaja? |
| ALEX | My dear [[characters/julia\|Julia]]!<br><br><br>It’s perfectly hopeless. You haven’t been listening. |
| PETER | You’ll have to tell us all over again, Alex. |
| ALEX | I never tell the same story twice. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | But I’m still waiting to know what happened.<br><br><br>I know it started as a story about tigers. |
| ALEX | I said there were no tigers. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Oh do stop wrangling,<br><br><br>Both of you. It’s your turn, [[characters/julia\|Julia]].<br>Do tell us that story you told the other day, about Lady Klootz and the wedding cake. |
| PETER | And how the butler found her in the pantry, rinsing her mouth out with champagne.<br><br><br>I like that story. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I [[motifs/love\|love]] that story. |
| ALEX | I’m never tired of hearing that story. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], you all seem to know it. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Do we all know it?<br><br><br>But we’re never tired of hearing you tell it.<br><br><br>I don’t believe everyone here knows it.<br>[To the UNIDENTIFIED GUEST] You don’t know it, do you? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | No, I’ve never heard it. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Here’s one new listener for you, [[characters/julia\|Julia]];<br>And I don’t believe that Edward knows it. |
| EDWARD | I may have heard it, but I don’t remember it. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | And [[characters/julia\|Julia]]’s the only person to tell it.<br><br><br>She’s such a good mimic. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Am I a good mimic? |
| PETER | You are a good mimic. You never miss anything. |
| ALEX | She never misses anything unless she wants to. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Especially the Lithuanian accent. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Lithuanian? Lady Klootz? |
| PETER | I thought she was Belgian. |
| ALEX | Her father belonged to a Baltic family —<br><br><br>One of the oldest Baltic families<br><br><br>With a branch in Sweden and one in [[settings/denmark\|Denmark]].<br>There were several very lovely daughters:<br>I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] what’s become of them now. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Lady Klootz was very lovely, once upon a [[motifs/time\|time]].<br><br><br>What a life she led! I used to say to her: ‘Greta!<br>You have too much vitality.’ But she enjoyed herself.<br>[To the UNIDENTIFIED GUEST] Did you know Lady Klootz? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | No, I never met her. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Go on with the story about the wedding cake. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], but it really isn’t my story.<br><br><br>I heard it first from Delia Verinder<br>Who was there when it happened.<br>[To the UNIDENTIFIED GUEST] Do you know Delia Verinder? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | No, I don’t know her. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], one can’t be too careful<br><br><br>Before one tells a story. |
| ALEX | Delia Verinder?<br><br><br>Was she the one who had three brothers? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | How many brothers? Two, I think. |
| ALEX | No, there were three, but you wouldn’t know the third one:<br><br><br>They kept him rather quiet. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, you mean that one. |
| ALEX | He was feeble-minded. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, not feeble-minded:<br><br><br>He was only harmless. |
| ALEX | [[motifs/well\|Well]] then, harmless. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | He was very clever at repairing clocks;<br><br><br>And he had a remarkable sense of hearing —<br>The only man I ever met who could hear the cry of bats. |
| PETER | Hear the cry of bats? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | He could hear the cry of bats. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But how do you know he could hear the cry of bats? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Because he said so. And I believed him. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But if he was so … harmless, how could you believe him?<br><br><br>He might have imagined it. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | My darling [[characters/celia\|Celia]],<br><br><br>You needn’t be so sceptical. I stayed there once<br>At their [[settings/castle\|castle]] in the North. How he suffered!<br>They had to find an island for him<br>Where there were no bats. |
| ALEX | And is he still there?<br><br><br>[[characters/julia\|Julia]] is really a mine of information. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | There isn’t much that [[characters/julia\|Julia]] doesn’t know. |
| PETER | Go on with the story about the wedding cake. |
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] the room] |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | No, we’ll wait until Edward comes back into the room.<br><br><br>Now I want to relax, Are there any more cocktails? |
| PETER | But do go on. Edward wasn’t listening anyway. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | No, he wasn’t listening, but he’s such a strain —<br><br><br>Edward without [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]! He’s quite impossible!<br>Leaving it to me to keep things going.<br>What a host! And nothing fit to eat!<br>The only reason for a cocktail party<br>For a gluttonous old woman like me<br>Is a really nice tit-bit. I can drink at [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD returns with a tray] |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward, give me another of those delicious olives.<br>What’s that? Potato crisps? No, I can’t endure them.<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], I started to tell you about Lady Klootz.<br>It was at the Vincewell wedding. Oh, so many years ago!<br>[To the UNIDENTIFIED GUEST] Did you know the Vincewells? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | No, I don’t know the Vincewells. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, they’re both dead now. But I wanted to know.<br><br><br>If they’d been friends of yours, I couldn’t tell the story. |
| PETER | Were they the parents of Tony Vincewell? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Yes. Tony was the product, but not the solution.<br><br><br>He only made the situation more difficult.<br>You know Tony Vincewell? You knew him at [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]? |
| PETER | No, I never knew him at [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]:<br><br><br>I came across him last year in California. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | I’ve always wanted to go to California.<br><br><br>Do tell us what you were doing in California. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Making a film. |
| PETER | Trying to make a film. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, what film was it? I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] if I’ve seen it. |
| PETER | No, you wouldn’t have seen it. As a matter of fact<br><br><br>It was never produced. They did a film<br>But they used a different scenario. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Not the one you wrote? |
| PETER | Not the one I wrote:<br><br><br>But I had a very enjoyable [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Go on with the story about the wedding cake. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward, do sit down for a moment.<br><br><br>I know you’re always the perfect host,<br>But just try to pretend you’re another guest<br>At [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s party. There are so many questions<br>I want to ask you. It’s a golden opportunity<br>Now [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s away. I’ve always said:<br>‘If I could only get Edward [[Alone\|alone]]<br>And have a really serious conversation!’<br><br><br>I said so to [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. She agreed with me.<br>She said: ‘I wish you’d try.’ And this is the first [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>I’ve ever seen you without [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br>Except for the [[motifs/time\|time]] she got locked in the lavatory<br>And couldn’t get out. I know what you’re thinking!<br>I know you think I’m a silly old woman<br>But I’m really very serious. [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] takes me seriously.<br>I believe that’s the reason why she went away —<br>So that I could make you talk. Perhaps she’s in the pantry<br>Listening to all we say! |
| EDWARD | No, she’s not in the pantry. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Will she be away for some [[motifs/time\|time]], Edward? |
| EDWARD | I really don’t know until I hear from her.<br><br><br>If her aunt is very ill, she may be gone some [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | And how will you manage while she is away? |
| EDWARD | I really don’t know. I may go away myself. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Go away yourself! |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Have you an aunt too? |
| EDWARD | No, I haven’t any aunt. But I might go away. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But, Edward … what was I going to say?<br><br><br>It’s dreadful for old ladies [[Alone\|alone]] in the country,<br>And almost impossible to get a [[characters/nurse\|nurse]]. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Is that her Aunt Laura? |
| EDWARD | No; another aunt<br><br><br>Whom you wouldn’t know. Her mother’s sister<br>And rather a recluse. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Her favourite aunt? |
| EDWARD | Her aunt’s favourite niece. And she’s rather difficult.<br><br><br>When she’s ill, she insists on having [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | I never heard of her being ill before. |
| EDWARD | No, she’s always very strong. That’s why when she’s ill<br><br><br>She gets into a panic. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | And sends for [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]].<br><br><br>I quite understand. Are there any prospects? |
| EDWARD | No, I think she put it all into an annuity. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | So it’s very unselfish of [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br><br><br>Yet very like her. But really, Edward,<br>[[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] may be away for weeks,<br>Or she may come back and be called away again.<br>I understand these tough old women —<br>I’m one myself. I feel as if I knew<br>All about that aunt in Hampshire. |
| EDWARD | Hampshire? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Didn’t you say Hampshire? |
| EDWARD | No, I didn’t say Hampshire. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Did you say Hampstead? |
| EDWARD | No, I didn’t say Hampstead. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | But she must live somewhere. |
| EDWARD | She lives in Essex. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Anywhere near Colchester? [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] loves oysters. |
| EDWARD | No. In the depths of Essex. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], we won’t probe into it.<br><br><br>You have the address, and the telephone number?<br>I might run down and see [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br>On my way to Cornwall. But let’s be sensible:<br>Now you must let me be your maiden aunt —<br><br><br>Living on an annuity, of course.<br>I am going to make you dine [[Alone\|alone]] with me<br>On Friday, and talk to me about everything. |
| EDWARD | Everything? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, you know what I mean.<br><br><br>The next election. And the [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]] of your cases. |
| EDWARD | Most of my [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]] are quite uninteresting. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], you shan’t escape. You dine with me on Friday.<br><br><br>I’ve already chosen the people you’re to meet. |
| EDWARD | But you asked me to dine with you [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Yes, [[Alone\|alone]]!<br><br><br>Without [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]! You’ll like the other people —<br>But you’re to talk to me. So that’s all settled.<br>And now I must be going. |
| EDWARD | Must you be going? |
| PETER | But won’t you tell the story about Lady Klootz? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | What Lady Klootz? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | And the wedding cake. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Wedding cake? I wasn’t at her wedding.<br><br><br>Edward, it’s been a delightful evening:<br>The potato crisps were really excellent.<br>Now let me see. Have I got everything?<br>It’s such a nice party, I hate to leave it.<br>It’s such a nice party, I’d like to repeat it.<br>Why don’t you all come to dinner on Friday?<br><br><br>No, I’m afraid my good Mrs. Batten<br>Would give me notice. And now I must be going. |
| ALEX | I’m afraid I ought to be going. |
| PETER | [[characters/celia\|Celia]] —<br><br><br>May I walk along with you? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | No, I’m sorry, Peter;<br><br><br>I’ve got to take a taxi. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | You come with me, Peter:<br><br><br>You can get me a taxi, and then I can drop you.<br><br><br>I expect you on Friday, Edward. And [[characters/celia\|Celia]] —<br>I must see you very soon. Now don’t all go<br>Just because I’m going. Good-bye, Edward. |
| EDWARD | Good-bye, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exeunt [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] and PETER] |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Good-bye, Edward.<br><br><br>Shall I see you soon? |
| EDWARD | Perhaps. I don’t know. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Perhaps you don’t know? Very [[motifs/well\|well]], good-bye. |
| EDWARD | Good-bye, [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |
| ALEX | Good-bye, Edward. I do [[concepts/hope\|hope]]<br><br><br>You’ll have better news of [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s aunt. |
| EDWARD | Oh … yes … thank you. Good-bye, Alex,<br><br><br>It was nice of you to come. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exeunt ALEX and [[characters/celia\|CELIA]]] |
| EDWARD | [To the UNIDENTIFIED GUEST] Don’t go yet.<br><br><br>Don’t go yet. We’ll finish the cocktails.<br>Or would you rather have whisky? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Gin. |
| EDWARD | Anything in it? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | A drop of [[motifs/water\|water]]. |
| EDWARD | I want to apologise for this evening.<br><br><br>The fact is, I tried to put off this party:<br>These were only the people I couldn’t put off<br>Because I couldn’t get at them in [[motifs/time\|time]];<br>And I didn’t know that you were coming.<br><br><br>I thought that [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] had told me the names<br>Of all the people she said she’d invited.<br>But it’s only that dreadful old woman who mattered —<br>I shouldn’t have minded anyone else, |
| *(didascalia)* | [The doorbell rings. EDWARD goes to the door, saying:] |
| EDWARD | But she always turns up when she’s least wanted. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Opens the door] |
| EDWARD | [[characters/julia\|Julia]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter [[characters/julia\|JULIA]]] |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward! How lucky that it’s raining!<br><br><br>It made me remember my umbrella,<br>And there it is! Now what are you two plotting?<br>How very lucky it was my umbrella,<br>And not Alexander’s — he’s so inquisitive!<br><br><br>But I never poke into other people’s business.<br><br><br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], good-bye again. I’m off at last. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| EDWARD | I’m sorry. I’m afraid I don’t know your name. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | I ought to be going. |
| EDWARD | Don’t go yet.<br><br><br>I very much want to talk to somebody;<br>And it’s easier to talk to a person you don’t know.<br>The fact is, that [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] has left me. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Your wife has left you? |
| EDWARD | Without warning, of course;<br><br><br>Just when she’d arranged a cocktail party.<br>She’d gone when I came in, this afternoon.<br>She left a note to say that she was leaving me;<br>But I don’t know where she’s gone. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | This is an occasion.<br><br><br>May I take another drink? |
| EDWARD | Whisky? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Gin. |
| EDWARD | Anything in it? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Nothing but [[motifs/water\|water]].<br><br><br>And I recommend you the same prescription …<br>Let me prepare it for you, if I may …<br>Strong … but sip it slowly … and drink it sitting down.<br>Breathe deeply, and adopt a relaxed position.<br>There we are. Now for a few questions.<br>How long married? |
| EDWARD | Five years. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Children? |
| EDWARD | No. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Then look at the brighter side.<br><br><br>You say you don’t know where she’s gone? |
| EDWARD | No, I do not. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Do you know who the man is? |
| EDWARD | There was no other man —<br><br><br>None that I know of. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Or another woman<br><br><br>Of whom she thought she had cause to be jealous? |
| EDWARD | She had nothing to complain of in my behaviour. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Then no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] it’s all for the best.<br><br><br>With another man, she might have made a mistake<br>And want to come back to you. If another woman,<br>She might decide to be forgiving<br>And gain an advantage. If there’s no other woman<br>And no other man, then the reason may be deeper<br>And you’ve ground for [[concepts/hope\|hope]] that she won’t come back at all.<br>If another man, then you’d want to re-marry<br>To prove to the world that somebody wanted you;<br>If another woman, you might have to marry her —<br>You might even imagine that you wanted to marry her. |
| EDWARD | But I want my wife back. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | That’s the natural reaction.<br><br><br>It’s embarrassing, and inconvenient.<br>It was inconvenient, having to lie about it<br>Because you can’t tell the [[concepts/truth\|truth]] on the telephone.<br>It will all take [[motifs/time\|time]] that you can’t [[motifs/well\|well]] spare;<br>But I put it to you … |
| EDWARD | Don’t put it to me. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Then I suggest … |
| EDWARD | And please don’t suggest.<br><br><br>I have often used these terms in examining witnesses,<br>So I don’t like them. May I put it to you?<br><br><br>I know that I invited this conversation:<br>But I don’t know who you are. This is not what I expected.<br>I only wanted to relieve my mind<br>By telling someone what I’d been concealing.<br>I don’t think I want to know who you are;<br>But, at the same [[motifs/time\|time]], unless you know my wife<br>A good deal better than I thought, or unless you know<br>A good deal more about us than appears —<br>I think your speculations rather offensive. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | I know you as [[motifs/well\|well]] as I know your wife;<br><br><br>And I knew that all you wanted was the luxury<br>Of an intimate disclosure to a stranger.<br>Let me, therefore, remain the stranger.<br>But let me tell you, that to approach the stranger<br>Is to invite the unexpected, release a new force,<br>Or let the genie out of the bottle.<br>It is to start a train of events<br>Beyond your control. So let me continue.<br>I will say then, you experience some relief<br>Of which you’re not aware. It will come to you slowly:<br>When you wake in the morning, when you go to bed at [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>That you are beginning to enjoy your independence;<br>Finding your life becoming cosier and cosier<br>Without the consistent critic, the patient misunderstander<br>Arranging life a little better than you like it,<br>Preferring not quite the same friends as yourself,<br>Or making your friends like her better than you;<br>And, turning the past over and over,<br>You’ll [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] only that you endured it for so long.<br>And perhaps at times you will feel a little jealous<br>That she saw it first, and had the [[concepts/courage\|courage]] to break it —<br>Thus giving herself a permanent advantage. |
| EDWARD | It might turn out so, yet … |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Are you going to say, you [[motifs/love\|love]] her? |
| EDWARD | Why, I thought we took each other for granted.<br><br><br>I never thought I should be any happier<br>With another person. Why speak of [[motifs/love\|love]]?<br>We were used to each other. So her going away<br>At a moment’s notice, without explanation,<br>Only a note to say that she had gone<br>And was not coming back — [[motifs/well\|well]], I can’t understand it.<br>Nobody likes to be left with a mystery:<br>It’s so … unfinished. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Yes, it’s unfinished;<br><br><br>And nobody likes to be left with a mystery.<br>But there’s more to it than that. There’s a loss of personality;<br>Or rather, you’ve lost touch with the person<br>You thought you were. You no longer feel quite human.<br>You’re suddenly reduced to the status of an object —<br>A living object, but no longer a person.<br>It’s always happening, because one is an object<br>As [[motifs/well\|well]] as a person. But we forget about it<br>As quickly as we can. When you’ve dressed for a party<br>And are going downstairs, with everything about you<br>Arranged to support you in the role you have chosen,<br>Then sometimes, when you come to the [[characters/bottom\|bottom]] step<br>There is one step more than your feet expected<br>And you come down with a jolt. Just for a moment<br>You have the experience of being an object<br>At the [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] of a malevolent staircase.<br>Or, take a surgical operation.<br>In consultation with the doctor and the surgeon,<br>In going to bed in the nursing [[motifs/home\|home]],<br>In talking to the matron, you are still the subject,<br>The centre of reality. But, stretched on the table,<br>You are a piece of furniture in a repair shop<br>For those who surround you, the masked actors;<br>All there is of you is your [[concepts/body\|body]]<br>And the ‘you’ is withdrawn. May I replenish? |
| EDWARD | Oh, I’m sorry. What were you drinking?<br><br><br>Whisky? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | Gin. |
| EDWARD | Anything with it? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | [[motifs/water\|Water]]. |
| EDWARD | To what does this lead? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | To finding out<br><br><br>What you really are. What you really feel.<br>What you really are among other people.<br>Most of the [[motifs/time\|time]] we take ourselves for granted,<br>As we have to, and live on a little knowledge<br>About ourselves as we were. Who are you now?<br>You don’t know any more than I do,<br>But rather less. You are nothing but a set<br>Of obsolete responses. The one thing to do<br>Is to do nothing. Wait. |
| EDWARD | Wait!<br><br><br>But waiting is the one thing impossible.<br>Besides, don’t you see that it makes me ridiculous? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | It will do you no harm to find yourself ridiculous.<br><br><br>Resign yourself to be the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] you are.<br>That’s the best advice that I can give you. |
| EDWARD | But how can I wait, not knowing what I’m waiting for?<br><br><br>Shall I say to my friends, ‘My wife has gone away’?<br>And they answer ‘Where?’ and I say ‘I don’t know’;<br>And they say, ‘But when will she be back?’<br>And I reply ‘I don’t know that she is coming back’.<br><br><br>And they ask ‘But what are you going to do?’<br>And I answer ‘Nothing’. They will think me mad<br>Or simply contemptible. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | All to the good.<br><br><br>You will find that you survive humiliation.<br>And that’s an experience of incalculable value. |
| EDWARD | Stop! I agree that much of what you’ve said<br><br><br>Is true enough. But that is not all.<br>Since I saw her this morning when we had breakfast<br>I no longer remember what my wife is like.<br>I am not quite sure that I could describe her<br>If I had to ask the police to search for her.<br>I’m sure I don’t know what she was wearing<br>When I saw her last. And yet I want her back.<br>And I must get her back, to find out what has happened<br><br><br>During the five years that we’ve been married.<br>I must find out who she is, to find out who I am.<br>And what is the use of all your analysis<br>If I am to remain always lost in the dark? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | There is certainly no purpose in remaining in the dark<br><br><br>Except long enough to clear from the mind<br>The illusion of having ever been in the light.<br>The fact that you can’t give a reason for wanting her<br>Is the best reason for believing that you want her. |
| EDWARD | I want to see her again — here. |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | You shall see her again — here. |
| EDWARD | Do you mean to say that you know where she is? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | That question is not worth the trouble of an answer.<br><br><br>But if I bring her back it must be on one condition:<br>That you promise to ask her no questions<br>Of where she has been. |
| EDWARD | I will not ask them.<br><br><br>And yet — it seems to me — when we began to talk<br>I was not sure I wanted her; and now I want her.<br>Do I want her? Or is it merely your suggestion? |
| UNIDENTIFIED GUEST | We do not know yet. In twenty-four hours<br><br><br>She will come to you here. You will be here to meet her. |
| *(didascalia)* | [The doorbell rings] |
| EDWARD | I must answer the door. |
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD goes to the door] |
| EDWARD |                                                          So it’s you again, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] and PETER] |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward, I’m so glad to find you.<br><br><br>Do you know, I must have left my glasses here,<br>And I simply can’t see a thing without them.<br>I’ve been dragging Peter all over town<br>Looking for them everywhere I’ve been.<br>Has anybody found them? You can tell if they’re mine —<br>Some kind of a plastic sort of frame —<br>I’m afraid I don’t remember the colour,<br>But I’d know them, because one lens is missing.<br>UNIDENTIFIED GUEST [Sings].<br>As I was drinkin’ gin and [[motifs/water\|water]],<br>And me bein’ the One Eyed Riley,<br>Who came in but the landlord’s daughter<br>And she took my heart entirely.<br>You will keep our appointment? |
| EDWARD | I shall keep it.<br><br><br>UNIDENTIFIED GUEST [Sings].<br>Tooryooly toory-iley,<br>What’s the matter with One Eyed Riley? |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward, who is that dreadful man?<br><br><br>I’ve never been so insulted in my life.<br>It’s very lucky that I left my spectacles:<br>This is what I call an adventure!<br><br><br>Tell me about him. You’ve been drinking together!<br><br><br>So this is the kind of friend you have<br>When [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] is out of the way! Who is he? |
| EDWARD | I don’t know. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | You don’t know? |
| EDWARD | I never saw him before in my life. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | But how did he come here? |
| EDWARD | I don’t know. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | You don’t know! And what’s his name?<br><br><br>Did I hear him say his name was Riley? |
| EDWARD | I don’t know his name. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | You don’t know his name? |
| EDWARD | I tell you I’ve no idea who he is<br><br><br>Or how he got here. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | But what did you talk about<br><br><br>Or were you singing [[songs]] all the [[motifs/time\|time]]?<br>There’s altogether too much mystery<br>About this place to-day. |
| EDWARD | I’m very sorry. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | No, I [[motifs/love\|love]] it. But that reminds me<br><br><br>About my glasses. That’s the greatest mystery.<br>Peter! Why aren’t you looking for them?<br>Look on the mantelpiece. Where was I sitting?<br>Just turn out the [[characters/bottom\|bottom]] of that sofa —<br>No, this chair. Look under the cushion. |
| EDWARD | Are you quite sure they’re not in your bag? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Why no, of course not: that’s where I keep them.<br><br><br>Oh, here they are! Thank you, Edward;<br>That really was very clever of you;<br>I’d never have found them but for you.<br>The next [[motifs/time\|time]] I lose anything, Edward,<br><br><br>I’ll come straight to you, instead of to St. Anthony.<br>And now I must fly. I’ve kept the taxi waiting.<br>Come along, Peter. |
| PETER | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] you won’t mind<br><br><br>If I don’t come with you, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]? On the way back<br>I remembered something I had to say to Edward … |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, about [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]? |
| PETER | No, not about [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]].<br><br><br>It’s something I want to consult him about,<br>And I could do it now. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Of course I don’t mind. |
| PETER | [[motifs/well\|Well]], at least you must let me take you down in the lift. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | No, you stop and talk to Edward. I’m not helpless yet.<br><br><br>And besides, I like to manage the [[motifs/machine\|machine]] myself —<br>In a lift I can meditate. Good-bye then.<br>And thank you — both of you — very much. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| PETER | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] I’m not disturbing you, Edward. |
| EDWARD | I seem to have been disturbed already;<br><br><br>And I did rather want to be [[Alone\|alone]].<br>But what’s it all about? |
| PETER | I want your help.<br><br><br>I was going to telephone and try to see you later;<br>But this seemed an opportunity. |
| EDWARD | And what’s your trouble? |
| PETER | This evening I felt I could bear it no longer.<br><br><br>That awful party! I’m sorry, Edward;<br>Of course it was really a very nice party<br>For everyone but me. And that wasn’t your fault.<br>I don’t suppose you noticed the situation. |
| EDWARD | I did think I noticed one or two things;<br><br><br>But I don’t pretend I was aware of everything. |
| PETER | Oh, I’m very glad that you didn’t notice:<br><br><br>I must have behaved rather better than I thought.<br>If you didn’t notice, I don’t suppose the others did,<br>Though I’m rather afraid of [[characters/julia\|Julia]] Shuttlethwaite. |
| EDWARD | [[characters/julia\|Julia]] is certainly observant,<br><br><br>But I think she had some other matter on her mind. |
| PETER | It’s about [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. Myself and [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |
| EDWARD | Why, what could there be about yourself and [[characters/celia\|Celia]]?<br><br><br>Have you anything in common, do you think? |
| PETER | It seemed to me we had a great deal in common.<br><br><br>We’re both of us artists. |
| EDWARD | I never thought of that.<br><br><br>What arts do you practise? |
| PETER | You won’t have seen my [[forms/novel\|novel]],<br><br><br>Though it had some very good reviews.<br>But it’s more the cinema that interests both of us. |
| EDWARD | A common interest in the moving pictures<br><br><br>Frequently brings young people together. |
| PETER | Now you’re only being sarcastic:<br><br><br>[[characters/celia\|Celia]] was interested in the art of the film. |
| EDWARD | As a possible profession? |
| PETER | She might make it a profession;<br><br><br>Though she had her poetry. |
| EDWARD | Yes, I’ve seen her poetry —<br><br><br>Interesting if one is interested in [[characters/celia\|Celia]].<br>Apart, of course, from its literary merit<br>Which I don’t pretend to judge. |
| PETER | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I can judge it,<br><br><br>And I think it’s very good. But that’s not the point.<br>The point is, I thought we had a great deal in common<br>And I think she thought so too. |
| EDWARD | How did you come to know her? |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter ALEX] |
| ALEX | Ah, there you are, Edward! Do you know why I’ve looked in? |
| EDWARD | I’d like to know first how you got in, Alex. |
| ALEX | Why, I came and found that the door was open<br><br><br>And so I thought I’d slip in and see if anyone was with you. |
| PETER | [[characters/julia\|Julia]] must have left it open. |
| EDWARD | Never mind;<br><br><br>So long as you both shut it when you go out. |
| ALEX | Ah, but you’re coming with me, Edward.<br><br><br>I thought, Edward may be all [[Alone\|alone]] this evening,<br>And I know that he hates to spend an evening [[Alone\|alone]],<br>So you’re going to come out and have dinner with me. |
| EDWARD | That’s very thoughtful of you, Alex, I’m sure;<br><br><br>But I rather want to be [[Alone\|alone]], this evening. |
| ALEX | But you’ve got to have some dinner. Are you going out?<br><br><br>Is there anyone here to get dinner for you? |
| EDWARD | No, I shan’t want much, and I’ll get it myself. |
| ALEX | Ah, in that case I know what I’ll do.<br><br><br>I’m going to give you a little surprise:<br>You know, I’m rather a famous cook.<br>I’m going straight to your kitchen now<br>And I shall prepare you a nice little dinner<br>Which you can have [[Alone\|alone]]. And then we’ll leave you.<br>Meanwhile, you and Peter can go on talking<br>And I shan’t disturb you. |
| EDWARD | My dear Alex,<br><br><br>There’ll be nothing in the larder worthy of your cooking.<br>I couldn’t think of it. |
| ALEX | Ah, but that’s my special gift —<br><br><br>Concocting a toothsome meal out of nothing.<br>Any scraps you have will do. I learned that in the East.<br>With a handful of rice and a little dried fish<br>I can make half a dozen dishes. Don’t say a word.<br>I shall begin at once. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit to kitchen] |
| EDWARD | [[motifs/well\|Well]], where did you leave off? |
| PETER | You asked me how I came to know [[characters/celia\|Celia]].<br><br><br>I met her here, about a year ago. |
| EDWARD | At one of [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s amateur Thursdays? |
| PETER | A [[thursday\|Thursday]]. Why do you say amateur? |
| EDWARD | [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s attempts at starting a salon,<br><br><br>Where I entertained the minor guests<br>And dealt with the misfits, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s mistakes.<br>But you were one of the minor successes<br>For a [[motifs/time\|time]] at least. |
| PETER | I wouldn’t say that.<br><br><br>But [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] was awfully kind to me<br>And I owe her a great deal. And then I met [[characters/celia\|Celia]].<br>She was different from any girl I’d ever known<br>And not easy to talk to, on that occasion. |
| EDWARD | Did you see her often?<br><br><br>ALEX’S VOICE. Edward, have you a [[topoi/double\|double]] boiler? |
| EDWARD | I suppose there must be a [[topoi/double\|double]] boiler:<br><br><br>Isn’t there one in every kitchen?<br>ALEX’S VOICE.                                 I can’t find it.<br><br><br>There goes that surprise. I must think of another. |
| PETER | Not very often.<br><br><br>And when I did, I got no chance to talk to her. |
| EDWARD | You and [[characters/celia\|Celia]] were asked for different purposes.<br><br><br>Your role was to be one of [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s discoveries;<br>[[characters/celia\|Celia]]’s, to provide society and fashion.<br>[[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] always had the [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]]<br>To establish herself in two worlds at once —<br>But she herself had to be the link between them.<br>That is why, I think, her Thursdays were a failure. |
| PETER | You speak as if everything was finished. |
| EDWARD | Oh no, no, everything is left unfinished.<br><br><br>But you haven’t told me how you came to know [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |
| PETER | I saw her again a few days later<br><br><br>[[works/alone-(poe)\|Alone]] at a concert. And I was [[Alone\|alone]].<br>I’ve always gone to concerts [[Alone\|alone]] —<br>At first, because I knew no one to go with,<br>And later, I found I preferred to go [[Alone\|alone]].<br>But a girl like [[characters/celia\|Celia]], it seemed very strange,<br>Because I thought of her merely as a name<br>In a society column, to find her there [[Alone\|alone]].<br>Anyway, we got into conversation<br>And I found that she went to concerts [[Alone\|alone]]<br>And to look at pictures. So we often met<br>In the same way, and sometimes went together.<br>And to be with [[characters/celia\|Celia]], that was something different<br>From company or [[concepts/solitude\|solitude]]. And we sometimes had tea<br>And once or twice dined together. |
| EDWARD | And after that<br><br><br>Did she ever introduce you to her family<br>Or to any of her friends? |
| PETER | No, but once or twice she spoke of them<br><br><br>And about their lack of intellectual interests. |
| EDWARD | And what happened after that? |
| PETER | Oh, nothing happened.<br><br><br>But I thought that she really cared about me.<br>And I was so happy when we were together —<br>So … contented, so … at peace: I can’t express it;<br>I had never imagined such quiet happiness.<br>I had only experienced excitement, delirium,<br>[[motifs/desire\|Desire]] for possession. It was not like that at all.<br>It was something very strange. There was such … tranquillity … |
| EDWARD | And what interrupted this interesting affair? |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter ALEX in shirtsleeves and an apron] |
| ALEX | Edward, I can’t find any curry powder. |
| EDWARD | There isn’t any curry powder. [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] hates curry. |
| ALEX | There goes another surprise, then. I must think.<br><br><br>I didn’t expect to find any mangoes,<br>But I did count upon curry powder. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit] |
| PETER | That is exactly what I want to know.<br><br><br>She has simply faded — into some other picture —<br>Like a film effect. She doesn’t want to see me;<br>Makes excuses, not very plausible,<br>And when I do see her, she seems preoccupied<br>With some secret excitement which I cannot share. |
| EDWARD | Do you think she has simply lost interest in you? |
| PETER | You put it just wrong. I think of it differently.<br><br><br>It is not her interest in me that I miss —<br><br><br>But those moments in which we seemed to share some perception,<br>Some feeling, some indefinable experience<br>In which we were both unaware of ourselves.<br>In your terms, perhaps, she’s lost interest in me. |
| EDWARD | That is all very normal. If you could only know<br><br><br>How lucky you are. In a little while<br>This might have become an ordinary affair<br>Like any other. As the fever cooled<br>You would have found that she was another woman<br>And that you were another man. I congratulate you<br>On a timely escape. |
| PETER | I should prefer to be spared<br><br><br>Your congratulations. I had to talk to someone.<br>And I have been telling you of something real —<br>My first experience of reality<br>And perhaps it is the last. And you don’t understand. |
| EDWARD | My dear Peter, I have only been telling you<br><br><br>What would have happened to you with [[characters/celia\|Celia]]<br>In another six months’ [[motifs/time\|time]]. There it is.<br>You can take it or leave it. |
| PETER | But what am I to do? |
| EDWARD | Nothing. Wait. Go back to California. |
| PETER | But I must see [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |
| EDWARD | Will it be the same [[characters/celia\|Celia]]?<br><br><br>Better be content with the [[characters/celia\|Celia]] you remember.<br>Remember! I say it’s already a [[motifs/memory\|memory]]. |
| PETER | But I must see [[characters/celia\|Celia]] at least to make her tell me<br><br><br>What has happened, in her terms. Until I know that<br>I shan’t know the [[concepts/truth\|truth]] about even the [[motifs/memory\|memory]].<br>Did we really share these interests? Did we really feel the same<br><br><br>When we heard certain music? Or looked at certain pictures?<br><br><br>There was something real. But what is the reality … |
| *(didascalia)* | [The telephone rings] |
| EDWARD | Excuse me a moment. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Into telephone] |
| EDWARD |                                                        Hello! … I can’t talk now …<br>Yes, there is … [[motifs/well\|Well]] then, I’ll ring you<br>As soon as I can.<br>[To PETER] I’m sorry. You were saying? |
| PETER | I was saying, what is the reality<br><br><br>Of experience between two unreal people?<br>If I can only hold to the [[motifs/memory\|memory]]<br>I can bear any future. But I must find out<br>The [[concepts/truth\|truth]] about the past, for the sake of the [[motifs/memory\|memory]]. |
| EDWARD | There’s no [[motifs/memory\|memory]] you can wrap in camphor<br><br><br>But the moths will get in. So you want to see [[characters/celia\|Celia]].<br>I don’t know why I should be taking all this trouble<br>To protect you from the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] you are.<br>What do you want me to do? |
| PETER | See [[characters/celia\|Celia]] for me.<br><br><br>You know her in a different way from me<br>And you are so much older. |
| EDWARD | So much older? |
| PETER | Yes, I’m sure that she would listen to you<br><br><br>As someone disinterested. |
| EDWARD | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I will see [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |
| PETER | Thank you, Edward. It’s very good of you. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter ALEX, with his jacket on] |
| ALEX | Oh, Edward! I’ve prepared you such a treat!<br><br><br>I really think that of all my triumphs<br>This is the greatest. To make something out of nothing!<br>Never, even when travelling in Albania,<br>Have I made such a supper out of so few materials<br>As I found in your refrigerator. But of course<br>I was lucky to find half-a-dozen eggs. |
| EDWARD | What! You used all those eggs! [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]’s aunt<br><br><br>Has just sent them from the country. |
| ALEX | Ah, so the aunt<br><br><br>Really exists. A substantial proof. |
| EDWARD | No, no … I mean, this is another aunt. |
| ALEX | I understand. The real aunt. But you’ll be grateful.<br><br><br>There are very few peasants in Montenegro<br>Who can have the dish that you’ll be eating, nowadays. |
| EDWARD | But what about my breakfast? |
| ALEX | Don’t worry about breakfast.<br><br><br>All you should want is a cup of black coffee<br>And a little dry toast. I’ve left it simmering.<br>Don’t leave it longer than another ten minutes.<br>Now I’ll be going, and I’ll take Peter with me. |
| PETER | Edward, I’ve taken too much of your [[motifs/time\|time]],<br><br><br>And you want to be [[Alone\|alone]]. Give my [[motifs/love\|love]] to [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br>When she comes back … but, if you don’t mind,<br>I’d rather you didn’t tell her what I’ve told you. |
| EDWARD | I shall not say anything about it to [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. |
| PETER | Thank you, Edward. Good [[motifs/night\|night]]. |
| EDWARD | Good [[motifs/night\|night]], Peter,<br><br><br>And good [[motifs/night\|night]], Alex. Oh, and if you don’t mind,<br>Please shut the door after you, so that it latches. |
| ALEX | Remember, Edward, not more than ten minutes, Twenty minutes, and my work will be ruined. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exeunt ALEX and PETER] |
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD picks up the telephone, and dials a number] |
| EDWARD | Is Miss [[characters/celia-coplestone\|Celia Coplestone]] in? … How long ago? …<br><br><br>No, it doesn’t matter.<br>CURTAIN |

| Chi parla | Battuta |  
|---|---|  
| *(didascalia)* | Il salotto dell'appartamento [[settings/london\|londinese]] dei Chamberlayne. Prima sera. [[characters/edward-chamberlayne\|EDWARD CHAMBERLAYNE]], [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] SHUTTLETHWAITE, [[characters/celia-coplestone\|CELIA COPLESTONE]], PETER QUILPE, ALEXANDER MACCOLGIE GIBBS, e un OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO. |  
| ALEX | Non hai capito affatto il punto, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]:<br><br><br>Non c'erano tigri. Questo era il punto. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | E allora che ci facevate, su un albero:<br><br><br>Tu e il Maharaja? |  
| ALEX | Mia cara [[characters/julia\|Julia]]!<br><br><br>È perfettamente inutile. Non hai ascoltato. |  
| PETER | Dovrai raccontarcelo daccapo, Alex. |  
| ALEX | Non racconto mai due volte la stessa storia. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Ma sto ancora aspettando di sapere che cosa è successo.<br><br><br>So che era cominciata come una storia di tigri. |  
| ALEX | Ho detto che non c'erano tigri. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Oh, smettetela di bisticciare,<br><br><br>Tutti e due. Tocca a te, [[characters/julia\|Julia]].<br>Raccontaci quella storia che hai raccontato l'altro giorno, di Lady Klootz e della torta nuziale. |  
| PETER | E di come il maggiordomo la trovò in dispensa, a sciacquarsi la bocca con lo champagne.<br><br><br>Mi piace quella storia. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | [[motifs/love\|Adoro]] quella storia. |  
| ALEX | Non mi stanco mai di sentire quella storia. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Bene]], pare che la conosciate tutti. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | La conosciamo tutti?<br><br><br>Ma non ci stanchiamo mai di sentirtela raccontare.<br><br><br>Non credo che qui tutti la conoscano.<br>[All'OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO] Lei non la conosce, vero? |  
| OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO | No, non l'ho mai sentita. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Ecco un nuovo ascoltatore per te, [[characters/julia\|Julia]];<br>E non credo che Edward la conosca. |  
| EDWARD | Può darsi che l'abbia sentita, ma non la ricordo. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | E [[characters/julia\|Julia]] è l'unica persona che sappia raccontarla.<br><br><br>È così brava a imitare. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Sono brava a imitare? |  
| PETER | Sei brava a imitare. Non ti sfugge mai nulla. |  
| ALEX | Non le sfugge mai nulla, a meno che non voglia. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Specialmente l'accento lituano. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Lituano? Lady Klootz? |  
| PETER | Credevo fosse belga. |  
| ALEX | Suo padre apparteneva a una famiglia baltica —<br><br><br>Una delle più antiche famiglie baltiche<br><br><br>Con un ramo in Svezia e uno in [[settings/denmark\|Danimarca]].<br>C'erano parecchie figlie assai graziose:<br>Mi [[concepts/wonder\|domando]] che ne sia stato di loro. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Lady Klootz era graziosissima, un [[motifs/time\|tempo]].<br><br><br>Che vita conduceva! Le dicevo sempre: «Greta!<br>Hai troppa vitalità». Ma si divertiva.<br>[All'OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO] Conosceva Lady Klootz? |  
| OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO | No, non l'ho mai incontrata. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Va' avanti con la storia della torta nuziale. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Beh]], ma in realtà non è una storia mia.<br><br><br>L'ho sentita per la prima volta da Delia Verinder<br>Che era là quando accadde.<br>[All'OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO] Conosce Delia Verinder? |  
| OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO | No, non la conosco. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Beh]], non si è mai troppo prudenti<br><br><br>Prima di raccontare una storia. |  
| ALEX | Delia Verinder?<br><br><br>Era quella che aveva tre fratelli? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Quanti fratelli? Due, credo. |  
| ALEX | No, erano tre, ma il terzo non lo conoscerai:<br><br><br>Lo tenevano piuttosto nascosto. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, intendi quello. |  
| ALEX | Era deficiente. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, non deficiente:<br><br><br>Era soltanto innocuo. |  
| ALEX | [[motifs/well\|Beh]] allora, innocuo. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Era bravissimo a riparare gli orologi;<br><br><br>E aveva un udito notevole —<br>L'unico uomo che abbia mai incontrato capace di udire il grido dei pipistrelli. |  
| PETER | Udire il grido dei pipistrelli? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Poteva udire il grido dei pipistrelli. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Ma come fai a sapere che poteva udire il grido dei pipistrelli? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Perché lo diceva lui. E io gli credevo. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Ma se era così… innocuo, come potevi credergli?<br><br><br>Potrebbe averlo immaginato. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Mia cara [[characters/celia\|Celia]],<br><br><br>Non c'è bisogno che tu sia così scettica. Una volta ci stetti<br>Nel loro [[settings/castle\|castello]] del Nord. Come soffriva!<br>Dovettero trovargli un'isola<br>Dove non ci fossero pipistrelli. |  
| ALEX | Ed è ancora là?<br><br><br>[[characters/julia\|Julia]] è davvero una miniera d'informazioni. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Non c'è molto che [[characters/julia\|Julia]] non sappia. |  
| PETER | Va' avanti con la storia della torta nuziale. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD [[motifs/leaves\|lascia]] la stanza] |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | No, aspetteremo che Edward torni nella stanza.<br><br><br>Ora voglio rilassarmi. Ci sono altri cocktail? |  
| PETER | Ma va' avanti. Tanto Edward non stava ascoltando. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | No, non stava ascoltando, ma è una tale fatica —<br><br><br>Edward senza [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]! È proprio impossibile!<br>Lasciare a me il compito di mandare avanti le cose.<br>Che padrone di casa! E niente di commestibile!<br>L'unica ragione d'un cocktail party<br>Per una vecchia golosa come me<br>È un bel bocconcino. Bere posso [[motifs/home\|a casa]]. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD torna con un vassoio] |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward, dammi un'altra di quelle olive deliziose.<br>Che cos'è? Patatine? No, non le sopporto.<br>[[motifs/well\|Dunque]], avevo cominciato a parlarvi di Lady Klootz.<br>Fu alle nozze dei Vincewell. Oh, tanti anni fa!<br>[All'OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO] Conosceva i Vincewell? |  
| OSPITE NON IDENTIFICATO | No, non conosco i Vincewell. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, ora sono morti tutti e due. Ma volevo saperlo.<br><br><br>Se fossero stati amici suoi, non potrei raccontare la storia. |  
| PETER | Erano i genitori di Tony Vincewell? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Sì. Tony fu il prodotto, ma non la soluzione.<br><br><br>Non fece che rendere la situazione più difficile.<br>Conosci Tony Vincewell? L'hai conosciuto a [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]? |  
| PETER | No, non l'ho mai conosciuto a [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]:<br><br><br>L'ho incontrato l'anno scorso in California. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Ho sempre desiderato andare in California.<br><br><br>Raccontaci che cosa facevi in California. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Girava un film. |  
| PETER | Cercavo di girare un film. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, che film era? Mi [[concepts/wonder\|domando]] se l'ho visto. |  
| PETER | No, non può averlo visto. In effetti<br><br><br>Non fu mai prodotto. Un film lo fecero<br>Ma usarono un'altra sceneggiatura. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Non quella che avevi scritto tu? |  
| PETER | Non quella che avevo scritto io:<br><br><br>Ma ho trascorso un [[motifs/time\|tempo]] assai piacevole. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Va' avanti con la storia della torta nuziale. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Edward, siediti un momento.<br><br><br>So che sei sempre il perfetto padrone di casa,<br>Ma prova a far finta d'essere un altro ospite<br>Alla festa di [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. Ci sono tante domande<br>Che voglio farti. È un'occasione d'oro<br>Ora che [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] è via. Ho sempre detto:<br>«Se solo potessi avere Edward [[Alone\|da solo]]<br>E fare una conversazione davvero seria!»<br><br><br>L'ho detto a [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. Era d'accordo con me.<br>Ha detto: «Vorrei che tu ci provassi». E questa è la prima [[motifs/time\|volta]]<br>Che ti vedo senza [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br>Tranne quella [[motifs/time\|volta]] che restò chiusa nel gabinetto<br>E non riusciva a uscire. So che cosa stai pensando!<br>So che mi credi una vecchia sciocca<br>Ma parlo davvero sul serio. [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] mi prende sul serio.<br>Credo sia questa la ragione per cui è andata via —<br>Perché io potessi farti parlare. Forse è in dispensa<br>Ad ascoltare tutto quel che diciamo! |  
| EDWARD | No, non è in dispensa. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Starà via per un po' di [[motifs/time\|tempo]], Edward? |  
| EDWARD | Non lo so davvero, finché non avrò sue notizie.<br><br><br>Se la zia è molto malata, può restare via un bel po' di [[motifs/time\|tempo]]. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | E come farai mentre è via? |  
| EDWARD | Non lo so davvero. Potrei andarmene via anch'io. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Andartene via anche tu! |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Hai una zia anche tu? |  
| EDWARD | No, non ho nessuna zia. Ma potrei andarmene. |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Ma, Edward… che cosa stavo per dire?<br><br><br>È terribile per le vecchie signore [[Alone\|sole]] in campagna,<br>E quasi impossibile trovare un'[[characters/nurse\|infermiera]]. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | È la sua zia Laura? |  
| EDWARD | No; un'altra zia<br><br><br>Che non conosceresti. La sorella di sua madre<br>E piuttosto una reclusa. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | La sua zia prediletta? |  
| EDWARD | La nipote prediletta della zia. Ed è piuttosto difficile.<br><br><br>Quando è malata, pretende assolutamente [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Non ho mai sentito che fosse malata, prima d'ora. |  
| EDWARD | No, è sempre fortissima. Ecco perché, quando è malata,<br><br><br>Le viene il panico. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | E manda a chiamare [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]].<br><br><br>Capisco perfettamente. Ci sono prospettive? |  
| EDWARD | No, credo che abbia investito tutto in un vitalizio. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Allora è molto altruista da parte di [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br><br><br>Eppure molto da lei. Ma davvero, Edward,<br>[[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] potrebbe restare via per settimane,<br>O tornare e poi essere richiamata via di nuovo.<br>Le conosco queste vecchie coriacee —<br>Ne sono una anch'io. Mi pare di sapere<br>Tutto di quella zia dell'Hampshire. |  
| EDWARD | Hampshire? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Non hai detto Hampshire? |  
| EDWARD | No, non ho detto Hampshire. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Hai detto Hampstead? |  
| EDWARD | No, non ho detto Hampstead. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Ma da qualche parte dovrà pur vivere. |  
| EDWARD | Vive nell'Essex. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Da qualche parte vicino a Colchester? [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]] adora le ostriche. |  
| EDWARD | No. Nel profondo dell'Essex. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Bene]], non ci ficcheremo il naso.<br><br><br>Hai l'indirizzo, e il numero di telefono?<br>Potrei fare un salto a trovare [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]<br>Andando in Cornovaglia. Ma siamo ragionevoli:<br>Ora devi lasciarmi fare la tua zia zitella —<br><br><br>Che vive di vitalizio, s'intende.<br>Ti farò cenare [[Alone\|da solo]] con me<br>Venerdì, e parlarmi di tutto. |  
| EDWARD | Di tutto? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh, sai che cosa intendo.<br><br><br>Le prossime elezioni. E i [[motifs/secrets\|segreti]] delle tue cause. |  
| EDWARD | La maggior parte dei miei [[motifs/secrets\|segreti]] è del tutto priva d'interesse. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Bene]], non scapperai. Ceni con me venerdì.<br><br><br>Ho già scelto le persone che devi conoscere. |  
| EDWARD | Ma mi avevi chiesto di cenare con te [[Alone\|da solo]]. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Sì, [[Alone\|da solo]]!<br><br><br>Senza [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]! Le altre persone ti piaceranno —<br>Ma devi parlare con me. Dunque è tutto stabilito.<br>E ora devo andare. |  
| EDWARD | Devi proprio andare? |  
| PETER | Ma non racconterai la storia di Lady Klootz? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Quale Lady Klootz? |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | E la torta nuziale. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Torta nuziale? Non ero alle sue nozze.<br><br><br>Edward, è stata una serata deliziosa:<br>Le patatine erano davvero eccellenti.<br>Ora vediamo. Ho preso tutto?<br>È una festa così bella, mi dispiace lasciarla.<br>È una festa così bella, vorrei ripeterla.<br>Perché non venite tutti a cena venerdì?<br><br><br>No, temo che la mia buona signora Batten<br>Mi darebbe le dimissioni. E ora devo andare. |  
| ALEX | Temo che dovrei andare anch'io. |  
| PETER | [[characters/celia\|Celia]] —<br><br><br>Posso accompagnarti a piedi? |  
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | No, mi dispiace, Peter;<br><br><br>Devo prendere un taxi. |

Perciò mi lasci continuare.<br>Dirò dunque che lei prova un certo sollievo<br>Di cui non è consapevole. Le verrà lentamente:<br>Quando si sveglia al mattino, quando va a letto la [[motifs/night\|notte]],<br>Che comincia a godere della sua indipendenza;<br>Trovando la sua vita sempre più comoda<br>Senza la critica costante, la paziente fraintenditrice<br>Che ordina la vita un po' meglio di quanto le piaccia,<br>Che preferisce amici non proprio uguali ai suoi,<br>O che fa sì che i suoi amici preferiscano sua moglie a lei;<br>E, rivoltando e rivoltando il passato,<br>Si [[concepts/wonder\|stupirà]] soltanto d'averlo sopportato tanto a lungo.<br>E forse a tratti si sentirà un poco geloso<br>Che sia stata sua moglie a vederlo per prima, e ad avere il [[concepts/courage\|coraggio]] di romperlo —<br>Assicurandosi così un vantaggio permanente. |
