| ALEX | No, [[characters/julia\|Julia]], il telegramma non lo possiamo spiegare. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sono certa che potresti spiegare il telegramma.<br><br><br>Non so perché. Ma mi pare che ieri<br>Io abbia avviato una [[motifs/machine\|macchina]], che continua a funzionare,<br>E non riesco a fermarla; no, non è come una [[motifs/machine\|macchina]] —<br>O se è una [[motifs/machine\|macchina]], è un altro a farla andare.<br>Ma chi? Qualcuno interferisce sempre…<br>Non mi sento libera… eppure sono stata io ad avviarla… |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Alex, credi che potremmo spiegare qualcosa? |  
| ALEX | Credo di no, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]. Deve scoprirlo da sé:<br><br><br>È l'unico modo. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Come hai ragione!<br><br><br>[[motifs/well\|Bene]], cari miei, ci vedremo prestissimo. |  
| EDWARD | Quando ti vedremo? |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Ho detto che mi vedrete?<br><br><br>Arrivederci. Credo… di non aver lasciato nulla. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra PETER] |  
| PETER | Ho trovato un taxi, [[characters/julia\|Julia]]. |  
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Splendido! Arrivederci! |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono [[characters/julia\|JULIA]], ALEX e PETER] |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Devo dire che non sembri molto contento di vedermi. |  
| EDWARD | Non posso dire d'aver avuto molte occasioni<br><br><br>Di sembrare alcunché. Ma naturalmente sono contento di vederti. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sì, è stata una cosa sciocca da dire.<br><br><br>Da collegiale. Come [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. Non so perché l'ho detto.<br>[[motifs/well\|Bene]], eccomi qua. |  
| EDWARD | Non devo fare domande. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | E so che non devo dare spiegazioni. |  
| EDWARD | E io non devo dare spiegazioni. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | E io non devo fare domande. Eppure… perché no? |  
| EDWARD | Non so perché no. E allora di che cosa parliamo? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | C'è una cosa che devo sapere, a causa degli altri<br><br><br>E di come regolarmi con loro. Riguarda quella festa.<br>Immagino non crederai che me n'ero completamente dimenticata!<br>Ti ho lasciato in un bell'impiccio. Che cosa hai fatto?<br>Me ne sono ricordata solo dopo essere partita. |  
| EDWARD | Ho telefonato a tutti quelli che sapevo dovessero venire<br><br><br>Ma non ho raggiunto tutti. E così alcuni sono venuti. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Chi è venuto? |  
| EDWARD | Proprio quelli che erano qui stasera… |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Curioso. |  
| EDWARD | …e un altro. Non so chi fosse,<br><br><br>Ma tu dovresti saperlo. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sì, credo di saperlo.<br><br><br>Ma [[characters/julia\|Julia]] mi lascia perplessa. Quella donna è il diavolo.<br>Sa per istinto quando sta per accadere qualcosa.<br>Fidati che non si perde una sola situazione imbarazzante!<br>E che cosa hai raccontato loro? |  
| EDWARD | Ho inventato una zia<br><br><br>Che stava male in campagna, e ti aveva mandata a chiamare. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Davvero, Edward! Avresti fatto meglio a dire la [[concepts/truth\|verità]]:<br><br><br>Nulla meno della [[concepts/truth\|verità]] avrebbe potuto ingannare [[characters/julia\|Julia]].<br>Ma come mai la zia è venuta ad abitare nell'Essex? |  
| EDWARD | [[characters/julia\|Julia]] mi ha costretto a farla abitare da qualche parte. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Capisco. Dunque [[characters/julia\|Julia]] l'ha fatta abitare nell'Essex;<br><br><br>E ha fatto arrivare i telegrammi dall'Essex.<br>[[motifs/well\|Bene]], dovrò dire a [[characters/julia\|Julia]] la [[concepts/truth\|verità]].<br>D'ora in poi dirò sempre la [[concepts/truth\|verità]].<br>Abbiamo sprecato tanto [[motifs/time\|tempo]] a mentire. |  
| EDWARD | Non capisco bene che cosa tu intenda. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, Edward!<br><br><br>Il punto è che, da quando sono stata via,<br>Vedo d'averti preso troppo sul serio.<br>E ora vedo quanto sei assurdo. |  
| EDWARD | È una conclusione molto seria<br><br><br>A cui essere giunta in… quante? … trentadue ore. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sì, una scoperta importantissima,<br><br><br>Scoprire d'aver passato cinque anni della tua vita<br>Con un uomo privo del senso dell'[[Humour\|umorismo]];<br>E che l'effetto su di me è stato<br>Che ho perso ogni senso dell'[[Humour\|umorismo]] io stessa.<br>Ecco che cosa è venuto dal cederti sempre. |  
| EDWARD | Non sapevo che tu mi avessi sempre ceduto.<br><br><br>A me è parso ben diverso. Già che siamo in argomento,<br>Credevo che fossi io ad aver ceduto a te. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | So che cosa intendi per cederti:<br><br><br>Intendi lasciare a me tutte le decisioni pratiche<br>Che avresti dovuto prendere tu. Ricordo —<br>Oh, avrei dovuto capire quel che si preparava —<br>Quando progettavamo il viaggio di nozze,<br>Non riuscivo a farti dire dove volessi andare… |  
| EDWARD | Ma volevo che fossi tu a prendere quella decisione. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Ma come potevo dire dove volevo andare<br><br><br>Se prima non proponevi tu qualche altro luogo?<br>E ricordo che alla fine, per disperazione,<br>Dissi: «Immagino che per te sia lo stesso andare a Peacehaven» —<br>E tu dicesti: «Per me va bene». |  
| EDWARD | Certo che andava bene.<br><br><br>Lo intendevo come un complimento. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Lo intendevi come un complimento!<br><br><br>Ed eri così pieno di riguardi, dicevano;<br>E credevi d'essere altruista. Era soltanto passività;<br>Volevi soltanto essere sorretto, incoraggiato…. |  
| EDWARD | Incoraggiato? A che cosa? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | A pensare [[motifs/well\|bene]] di te stesso.<br><br><br>Sai che fui io a farti lavorare al foro… |  
| EDWARD | Mi assillavi perché non avevo abbastanza lavoro<br><br><br>E dicevi che dovevo conoscere più gente:<br>Ma quando le cause cominciarono ad arrivare —<br>E non arrivavano attraverso nessuno dei tuoi amici —<br><br><br>D'un tratto trovasti scomodo<br>Che fossi sempre troppo occupato o troppo stanco<br>Per esserti utile in società… |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Non mi sono mai lamentata. |  
| EDWARD | No; ed era perfettamente esasperante,<br><br><br>Il modo in cui non ti lamentavi… |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Eri tu a lamentarti<br><br><br>Di non vedere che procuratori e clienti… |  
| EDWARD | E tu non sei mai stata molto comprensiva. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Beh]], ma qualcosa ho cercato di fare.<br><br><br>Fu per questo che mi presi tanta pena<br>D'organizzare quei giovedì, per darti la possibilità<br>Di parlare con gente intellettuale… |  
| EDWARD | Mi avresti dato all'incirca la stessa occasione<br><br><br>Se mi avessi assunto come maggiordomo:<br>Qualcuno dei tuoi ospiti mi avrà creduto il maggiordomo. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | E in parecchie occasioni, quando doveva venire qualcuno<br><br><br>Che tenevo particolarmente a farti conoscere,<br>Arrivavi soltanto quando quello se ne stava andando. |  
| EDWARD | [[motifs/well\|Bene]], almeno non possono avermi creduto il maggiordomo. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Tutto ciò che tentavo non faceva che peggiorare le cose,<br><br><br>E nel momento in cui ti si offriva ciò che volevi<br>Ne volevi un altro. Ti tratterò in modo molto diverso<br>D'ora in avanti. |  
| EDWARD | Grazie dell'avvertimento. Ma dimmi,<br><br><br>Se è così che mi vedi, perché sei tornata? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Francamente, non lo so. Ero stata avvertita del pericolo,<br><br><br>Eppure qualcosa, o qualcuno, mi ha costretta a venire.<br>E tu perché mi volevi? |  
| EDWARD | Non lo so neppure io.<br><br><br>Dici che cercavi d'«incoraggiarmi»:<br>E allora perché mi facevi sempre sentire insignificante?<br>Può darsi che non sapessi quale vita volessi,<br>Ma non era la vita che tu avevi scelto per me.<br>Volevi che tuo marito avesse successo,<br>Volevi che io fornissi uno sfondo pubblico<br>Alla tua vita pubblica. Volevi essere una padrona di casa<br>Per la quale la mia carriera fosse un sostegno.<br>[[motifs/well\|Bene]], ho cercato d'essere accomodante. Ma, in futuro,<br>Mi comporterò, te l'assicuro, in modo molto diverso. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Bravo! Edward. Questo sorprende.<br><br><br>Ora, chi può averti insegnato a ribattere così? |  
| EDWARD | Ho avuto abbastanza umiliazioni<br>Ultimamente, da giungere al punto<br>In cui l'umiliazione cessa di umiliare.<br>Si arriva al punto in cui si cessa di sentire<br>E allora si dice quel che si pensa. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sarà una novità<br><br><br>Scoprire che hai un pensiero da dire.<br>Comunque sia, sono disposta a prenderti come sei. |  
| EDWARD | Vuoi dire che sei disposta a prendermi<br><br><br>Come ero, o come credi che io sia.<br>Ma che cosa credi che io sia? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, quello che sei sempre stato.<br><br><br>Quanto a me, sono una persona piuttosto diversa<br>Che tu devi imparare a conoscere. |  
| EDWARD | Molto interessante:<br><br><br>Ma sembri dare per scontato d'essere tu la sola a cambiare —<br>Anche se finora non l'ho trovato un cambiamento in meglio.<br>Ma non ti viene in mente che forse<br>Anch'io possa essere cambiato? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, Edward, quando eri bambino,<br><br><br>Sono certa che ti facevi sempre misurare<br>Per dimostrare quanto eri cresciuto dalle vacanze precedenti.<br>Sei sempre stato intensamente occupato di te stesso;<br>E se gli altri crescono, [[motifs/well\|beh]], vuoi crescere anche tu.<br>In che modo saresti cambiato? |  
| EDWARD | Il cambiamento che viene<br><br><br>Dal vedersi con gli occhi degli altri. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Dev'essere stato sconvolgente per te.<br><br><br>Ma non importa, presto ti passerà<br>E ti troverai un'altra piccola parte da recitare,<br>Con un'altra faccia, per ingannare la gente. |  
| EDWARD | Una delle cose più esasperanti di te<br><br><br>È sempre stata la tua perfetta sicurezza<br>Di capirmi meglio di quanto io capissi me stesso. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | E la cosa più esasperante di te<br><br><br>È sempre stata la tua placida convinzione<br>Che non valessi la pena d'essere capita. |  
| EDWARD | Eccoci dunque daccapo. Di nuovo nella trappola,<br><br><br>Con una sola differenza, forse — possiamo azzuffarci,<br><br><br>Invece di starcene ciascuno nel proprio angolo della gabbia.<br>[[motifs/well\|Beh]], è un modo migliore di passare la serata<br>Che ascoltare il grammofono. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Abbiamo ottimi dischi;<br><br><br>Ma ho sempre sospettato che tu detestassi davvero la musica<br>E che il grammofono fosse soltanto la tua via di fuga<br>Dal parlare con me quando dovevamo restare [[Alone\|soli]]. |  
| EDWARD | Mi sono spesso chiesto perché mi hai sposato. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Beh]], eri davvero piuttosto attraente, sai;<br><br><br>E continuavi a dire che provavi [[motifs/love\|amore]] per me —<br><br><br>Credo che cercassi di persuaderti che era vero.<br>Mi pareva d'essere sempre sul punto d'una qualche esperienza meravigliosa<br>E poi non accadeva mai. Ora mi [[concepts/wonder\|domando]]<br>Come tu abbia potuto credere di provare [[motifs/love\|amore]] per me. |  
| EDWARD | Tutti mi dicevano che era così;<br><br><br>E mi dicevano quanto [[motifs/well\|bene]] fossimo assortiti. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Peccato che tu non avessi un'opinione tua.<br><br><br>Oh, Edward, vorrei essere buona con te —<br>O se questo è impossibile, almeno essere odiosa con te —<br>Qualunque cosa fuorché nulla, che è tutto ciò che sembri volere da me.<br>Ma mi fai pena… |  
| EDWARD | Non dire che ti faccio pena!<br><br><br>Ne ho abbastanza di gente cui faccio pena. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sì, perché non potranno mai farti tanta pena<br><br><br>Quanta te ne fai tu stesso. Ed è duro da sopportare.<br>Pensavo che potesse esserci una via d'uscita per te<br>Se me ne fossi andata. Pensavo che se io fossi morta<br>Per te, io che per te ero stata soltanto un [[archetypes/ghost\|fantasma]],<br>Tu avresti potuto ritrovare la [[motifs/road\|strada]] del ritorno<br>A un [[motifs/time\|tempo]] in cui eri reale — perché devi essere stato reale<br>In un [[motifs/time\|tempo]] o nell'altro, prima ancora di conoscermi:<br>Forse soltanto quando eri [[archetypes/child\|bambino]]. |  
| EDWARD | Non voglio che tu ti renda responsabile di me:<br><br><br>È soltanto un'altra forma di disprezzo.<br>E non voglio che tu mi spieghi a me stesso.<br>Stai ancora cercando d'inventarmi una personalità<br>Che non farà che tenermi lontano da me stesso. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Stai complicando ciò che in realtà è semplicissimo.<br><br><br>Ma c'è un punto che vedo chiaramente:<br>Non dobbiamo ricadere nel genere di vita che conducevamo<br>Fino a ieri mattina. |  
| EDWARD | C'era una porta<br><br><br>E non riuscivo ad aprirla. Non riuscivo a toccare la maniglia.<br>Perché non potevo uscire dalla mia [[motifs/prison\|prigione]]?<br>Che cos'è l'inferno? L'inferno è se stessi,<br>L'inferno è [[Alone\|solitudine]], le altre figure che vi stanno<br>Semplici proiezioni. Non c'è nulla da cui fuggire<br>E nulla verso cui fuggire. Si è sempre [[Alone\|soli]]. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Edward, di che cosa stai parlando?<br><br><br>Parli a te stesso. Potresti, per un momento,<br>Pensare a me? |  
| EDWARD | È stato soltanto ieri<br><br><br>Che è avvenuta la dannazione. E ora devo conviverci<br>Giorno per giorno, ora per ora, nei secoli dei secoli. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Credo che tu sia sull'orlo d'un esaurimento nervoso! |  
| EDWARD | Non dirlo! |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Devo dirlo.<br><br><br>Conosco… un dottore che credo potrebbe aiutarti. |  
| EDWARD | Se andrò da un dottore, lo sceglierò io;<br><br><br>Non ne prenderò uno scelto da te. Come faccio a sapere<br>Che non lo vedresti prima tu, per raccontargli tutto di me<br>Dal tuo punto di vista? Ma non ho bisogno d'un dottore.<br><br><br>Sono semplicemente all'inferno. Dove non ci sono dottori —<br>O almeno, non in veste professionale. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Si può essere pratici anche all'inferno:<br><br><br>E sai che io sono molto più pratica di te. |  
| EDWARD | Ormai dovrei sapere che cosa consideri pratico.<br><br><br>Pratica! Ricordo che, in viaggio di nozze,<br>Avvolgevi sempre tutto nella carta velina<br>E poi dovevi scartare tutto daccapo<br>Per trovare quello che ti serviva. E non sono mai riuscito a insegnarti<br>Come si rimette il tappo su un tubetto di dentifricio. |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Molto [[motifs/well\|bene]], allora, non cercherò d'insistere.<br><br><br>Sei troppo diviso per sapere che cosa vuoi.<br>Ma, essendo diviso, tenderai al compromesso,<br>E il tuo genere di compromesso sarà quello di sempre. |  
| EDWARD | Non mi capisci. Non l'ho detto chiaramente<br><br><br>Che in futuro mi troverai una persona diversa? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Davvero. E la differenza non ha nulla a che fare<br><br><br>Con [[characters/celia\|Celia]] che parte per la California? |  
| EDWARD | [[characters/celia\|Celia]]? Che parte per la California? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Sì, con Peter.<br><br><br>Davvero, Edward, se fossi umano<br>Scoppieresti a ridere. Ma non lo farai. |  
| EDWARD | O Dio, o Dio, se potessi tornare a ieri<br><br><br>Prima di credere d'aver preso una decisione.<br>Quale demonio ha lasciato la porta socchiusa<br>Perché entrassero questi dubbi? E poi sei tornata tu,<br>L'angelo della distruzione — proprio come sentivo di certo.<br>In un attimo, al tuo tocco, non resta che rovina.<br>O Dio, che cosa ho fatto? Il pitone. La piovra.<br>Devo alla fine diventare ciò che tu vorresti farmi? |  
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Bene]], Edward, poiché non riesco a farti ridere,<br><br><br>E poiché non riesco a convincerti a vedere un dottore,<br>Per ora non posso farci altro.<br>Dovrei andare a dare un'occhiata in cucina.<br>So che ci sono delle uova. Ma dobbiamo uscire a cena.<br>Intanto, il mio bagaglio è nell'ingresso al pianterreno:<br>Vuoi dire al portiere di portarmelo su?<br>SIPARIO |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_2--scene_1" data-title="The Cocktail Party — Act II" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/double,archetype/child,archetype/fool,archetype/lover,archetype/shadow,motif/childhood,motif/clock,motif/death,motif/desert,motif/desire,motif/dreams,motif/fire,motif/home,motif/journey,motif/leaves,motif/love,motif/memory,motif/mirror,motif/moon,motif/nature,motif/pain,motif/prison,motif/road,motif/silence,motif/stars,motif/time,motif/well,concept/body,concept/conscience,concept/courage,concept/despair,concept/doubt,concept/faith,concept/fear,concept/freedom,concept/greed,concept/history,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/imagination,concept/loneliness,concept/nature,concept/self,concept/solitude,concept/suffering,concept/vanity,concept/wonder,form/history,setting/court,setting/desert,setting/london,character/celia,character/celia_coplestone,character/julia,character/lavinia,character/nurse,character/sir_henry_harcourt_reilly"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/sir-henry-harcourt-reilly\|SIR HENRY HARCOURT-REILLY]]’S consulting room in [[settings/london\|London]]. Morning: several weeks later. SIR HENRY [[Alone\|alone]] at his desk. He presses an electric button. The [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY enters, with Appointment Book. |
| REILLY | About those three appointments this morning, Miss Barraway:<br><br><br>I should like to run over my instructions again.<br>You understand, of course, that it is important<br>To avoid any meeting? |
| [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY | You made that clear, Sir Henry:<br><br><br>The first appointment at eleven o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]].<br>He is to be shown into the small waiting-room;<br>And you will see him almost at once. |
| REILLY | I shall see him at once. And the second? |
| [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY | The second to be shown into the other room<br><br><br>Just as usual. She arrives at a quarter past;<br>But you may keep her waiting. |
| REILLY | Or she may keep me waiting;<br><br><br>But I think she will be punctual. |
| [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY | I telephone through<br><br><br>The moment she arrives. I leave her there<br>Until you ring three times. |
| REILLY | And the third patient? |
| [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY | The third one to be shown into the small room;<br><br><br>And I need not let you know that she has arrived.<br>Then, when you ring, I show the others out;<br>And only after they have left the [[house]]…. |
| REILLY | Quite right, Miss Barraway. That’s all for the moment. |
| [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY | Mr. Gibbs is here, Sir Henry. |
| REILLY | Ask him to come straight in. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY] |
| *(didascalia)* | [ALEX enters almost immediately] |
| ALEX | When is Chamberlayne’s appointment? |
| REILLY | At eleven o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]],<br><br><br>The conventional hour. We have not much [[motifs/time\|time]].<br>Tell me now, did you have any difficulty<br>In convincing him I was the man for his case? |
| ALEX | Difficulty? No! He was only impatient<br><br><br>At having to wait four days for the appointment. |
| REILLY | It was necessary to delay his appointment<br><br><br>To lower his resistance. But what I mean is,<br>Does he trust your judgement? |
| ALEX | Yes, implicitly.<br><br><br>It’s not that he regards me as very intelligent,<br>But he thinks I’m [[motifs/well\|well]] informed: the sort of person<br>Who would know the right doctor, as [[motifs/well\|well]] as the right shops.<br><br><br>Besides, he was ready to consult any doctor<br>Recommended by anyone except his wife. |
| REILLY | I had already impressed upon her<br><br><br>That she was not to mention my name to him. |
| ALEX | With your usual foresight. Now, he’s quite triumphant<br><br><br>Because he thinks he’s stolen a march on her.<br>And when you’ve sent him to a sanatorium<br>Where she can’t get at him — then, he believes,<br>She will be very penitent. He’s enjoying his illness. |
| REILLY | Illness offers him a [[topoi/double\|double]] advantage:<br><br><br>To escape from himself — and get the better of his wife. |
| ALEX | Not to escape from her? |
| REILLY | He doesn’t want to escape from her. |
| ALEX | He is staying at his club. |
| REILLY | Yes, that is where he wrote from. |
| *(didascalia)* | [The [[house]]-telephone rings] |
| REILLY | Hello! Yes, show him up. |
| ALEX | You will have a busy morning!<br><br><br>I will go out by the service staircase<br>And come back when they’ve gone. |
| REILLY | Yes, when they’ve gone. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit ALEX by side door] |
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD is shown in by [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY] |
| EDWARD | [[characters/sir-henry-harcourt-reilly\|Sir Henry Harcourt-Reilly]] — |
| *(didascalia)* | [Stops and stares at REILLY] |
| EDWARD | REILLY [without looking up from his papers]. Good morning, Mr. Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>Please sit down. I won’t keep you a moment.<br>— Now, Mr. Chamberlayne? |
| EDWARD | It came into my mind<br><br><br>Before I entered the door, that you might be the same person:<br>But I dismissed that as just another symptom.<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], I should have known better than to come here<br>On the recommendation of a man who did not know you.<br>Yet Alex is so plausible. And his recommendations<br>Of shops, have always been satisfactory.<br>I beg your pardon. But he is a blunderer.<br><br><br>I should like to know … but what is the use!<br>I suppose I might as [[motifs/well\|well]] go away at once. |
| REILLY | No. If you please, sit down, Mr. Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>You are not going away, so you might as [[motifs/well\|well]] sit down.<br>You were going to ask a question. |
| EDWARD | When you came to my flat<br><br><br>Had you been invited by my wife as a guest<br>As I supposed? … Or did she send you? |
| REILLY | I cannot say that I had been invited;<br><br><br>And Mrs. Chamberlayne did not know that I was coming.<br>But I knew you would be there, and whom I should find with you. |
| EDWARD | But you had seen my wife? |
| REILLY | Oh yes, I had seen her. |
| EDWARD | So this is a trap! |
| REILLY | Let’s not call it a trap.<br><br><br>But if it is a trap, then you cannot escape from it:<br>And so … you might as [[motifs/well\|well]] sit down.<br>I think that you will find that chair comfortable. |
| EDWARD | You knew,<br><br><br>Before I began to tell you, what had happened? |
| REILLY | That is so, that is so. But all in good [[motifs/time\|time]].<br><br><br>Let us dismiss that question for the moment.<br>Tell me first, about the difficulties<br>On which you want my professional opinion. |
| EDWARD | It’s not for me to blame you for bringing my wife back,<br><br><br>I suppose. You seemed to be trying to persuade me<br>That I was better off without her. But didn’t you realise<br>That I was in no state to make a decision? |
| REILLY | If I had not brought your wife back, Mr. Chamberlayne,<br><br><br>Do you suppose that things would be any better — now? |
| EDWARD | I don’t know, I’m sure. They could hardly be worse. |
| REILLY | They might be much worse. You might have ruined three lives<br><br><br>By your indecision. Now there are only two —<br>Which you still have the chance of redeeming from ruin. |
| EDWARD | You talk as if I was capable of action:<br><br><br>If I were, I should not need to consult you<br>Or anyone else. I came here as a patient.<br>If you take no interest in my case, I can go elsewhere. |
| REILLY | You have reason to believe that you are very ill? |
| EDWARD | I should have thought a doctor could see that for himself.<br><br><br>Or at least that he would enquire about the symptoms.<br>Two people advised me recently,<br>Almost in the same words, that I ought to see a doctor.<br>They said — again, in almost the same words —<br>That I was on the edge of a nervous breakdown.<br>I didn’t know it then myself — but if they saw it<br>I should have thought that a doctor could see it. |
| REILLY | ‘Nervous breakdown’ is a term I never use:<br><br><br>It can mean almost anything. |
| EDWARD | And since then, I have realised<br><br><br>That mine is a very unusual case. |
| REILLY | All cases are unique, and very similar to others. |
| EDWARD | Is there a sanatorium to which you send such patients<br><br><br>As myself, under your personal observation? |
| REILLY | You are very impetuous, Mr. Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>There are several kinds of sanatoria<br>For several kinds of patient. And there are also patients<br>For whom a sanatorium is the worst place possible.<br>We must first find out what is wrong with you<br>Before we decide what to do with you. |
| EDWARD | I [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] if you have ever had a case like mine:<br><br><br>I have ceased to believe in my own personality. |
| REILLY | Oh, dear yes; this is serious. A very common malady.<br><br><br>Very prevalent indeed. |
| EDWARD | I remember, in my [[motifs/childhood\|childhood]] … |
| REILLY | I always begin from the immediate situation<br><br><br>And then go back as far as I find necessary.<br>You see, your memories of [[motifs/childhood\|childhood]] —<br>I mean, in your present state of mind —<br>Would be largely fictitious; and as for your [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]],<br>You would produce amazing [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]], to oblige me.<br>I could make you dream any kind of dream I suggested,<br>And it would only go to flatter your [[concepts/vanity\|vanity]]<br>With the temporary stimulus of feeling interesting. |
| EDWARD | But I am obsessed by the thought of my own insignificance. |
| REILLY | Precisely. And I could make you feel important,<br><br><br>And you would imagine it a marvellous cure;<br>And you would go on, doing such amount of mischief<br>As lay within your power — until you came to grief.<br>Half of the harm that is done in this world<br>Is due to people who want to feel important.<br>They don’t mean to do harm — but the harm does not interest them.<br><br><br>Or they do not see it, or they justify it<br>Because they are absorbed in the endless struggle<br>To think [[motifs/well\|well]] of themselves. |
| EDWARD | If I am like that<br><br><br>I must have done a great deal of harm. |
| REILLY | Oh, not so much as you would like to think:<br><br><br>Only, shall we say, within your modest capacity.<br>Try to explain what has happened since I left you. |
| EDWARD | I see now why I wanted my wife to come back.<br><br><br>It was because of what she had made me into.<br>We had not been [[Alone\|alone]] again for fifteen minutes<br>Before I felt, and still more acutely —<br>Indeed, acutely, perhaps, for the first [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>The whole oppression, the unreality<br>Of the role she had always imposed upon me<br>With the obstinate, unconscious, sub-human strength<br>That some women have. Without her, it was vacancy.<br>When I thought she had left me, I began to dissolve,<br>To cease to exist. That was what she had done to me!<br>I cannot live with her — that is now intolerable;<br>I cannot live without her, for she has made me incapable<br>Of having any existence of my own.<br>That is what she has done to me in five years together!<br>She has made the world a place I cannot live in<br>Except on her terms. I must be [[Alone\|alone]],<br>But not in the same world. So I want you to put me<br>Into your sanatorium. I could be [[Alone\|alone]] there? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[[house\|House]]-telephone rings] |
| EDWARD | REILLY [into telephone]. Yes.<br><br><br>[To EDWARD] Yes, you could be [[Alone\|alone]] there. |
| EDWARD | I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]<br><br><br>If you have understood a word of what I have been saying. |
| REILLY | You must have patience with me, Mr. Chamberlayne:<br><br><br>I learn a good deal by merely observing you,<br>And letting you talk as long as you please,<br>And taking note of what you do not say. |
| EDWARD | I once experienced the extreme of physical [[motifs/pain\|pain]],<br><br><br>And now I know there is [[concepts/suffering\|suffering]] worse than that.<br>It is surprising, if one had [[motifs/time\|time]] to be surprised:<br>I am not afraid of the [[motifs/death\|death]] of the [[concepts/body\|body]],<br>But this [[motifs/death\|death]] is terrifying. The [[motifs/death\|death]] of the spirit —<br>Can you understand what I suffer? |
| REILLY | I understand what you mean. |
| EDWARD | I can no longer act for myself.<br><br><br>Coming to see you — that’s the last decision<br>I was capable of making. I am in your hands.<br>I cannot take any further responsibility. |
| REILLY | Many patients come in that belief. |
| EDWARD | And now will you send me to the sanatorium? |
| REILLY | You have nothing else to tell me? |
| EDWARD | What else can I tell you?<br><br><br>You didn’t want to hear about my early [[concepts/history\|history]]. |
| REILLY | No, I did not want to hear about your early [[concepts/history\|history]]. |
| EDWARD | And so will you send me to the sanatorium?<br><br><br>I can’t go [[motifs/home\|home]] again. And at my club<br>They won’t let you keep a room for more than seven days;<br>I haven’t the [[concepts/courage\|courage]] to go to a hotel,<br>And besides, I need more shirts — you can get my wife<br>To have my things sent on: whatever I shall need.<br>But of course you mustn’t tell her where I am.<br>Is it far to go? |
| REILLY | You might say, a long [[motifs/journey\|journey]].<br><br><br>But before I treat a patient like yourself<br>I need to know a great deal more about him,<br>Than the patient himself can always tell me.<br>Indeed, it is often the case that my patients<br>Are only pieces of a total situation<br>Which I have to explore. The single patient<br>Who is ill by himself, is rather the exception.<br>I have recently had another patient<br>Whose situation is much the same as your own. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Presses the bell on his desk three times] |
| REILLY | You must accept a rather unusual procedure:<br>I propose to introduce you to the other patient. |
| EDWARD | What do you mean? Who is this other patient?<br><br><br>I consider this very unprofessional conduct —<br>I will not discuss my case before another patient. |
| REILLY | On the contrary. That is the only way<br><br><br>In which it can be discussed. You have told me nothing.<br>You have had the opportunity, and you have said enough<br>To convince me that you have been making up your case<br>So to speak, as you went along. A barrister<br>Ought to know his brief before he enters the [[settings/court\|court]]. |
| EDWARD | I am at least free to leave. And I propose to do so.<br><br><br>My mind is made up. I shall go to a hotel. |
| REILLY | It is just because you are not free, Mr. Chamberlayne,<br><br><br>That you have come to me. It is for me to give you that —<br>Your [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]]. That is my affair. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] is shown in by the [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY] |
| REILLY | But here is the other patient. |
| EDWARD | [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]! |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], Sir Henry!<br><br><br>I said I would come to talk about my husband:<br>I didn’t say I was prepared to meet him. |
| EDWARD | And I did not expect to meet you, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]].<br><br><br>I call this a very dishonourable trick. |
| REILLY | Honesty before [[concepts/honour\|honour]], Mr. Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>Sit down, please, both of you. Mrs. Chamberlayne,<br>Your husband wishes to enter a sanatorium,<br>And that is a question which naturally concerns you. |
| EDWARD | I am not going to any sanatorium.<br><br><br>I am going to a hotel. And I shall ask you, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]],<br>To be so good as to send me on some clothes. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Oh, to what hotel? |
| EDWARD | I don’t know — I mean to say,<br><br><br>That doesn’t concern you. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | In that case, Edward,<br><br><br>I don’t think your clothes concern me either.<br>[To REILLY] I presume you will send him to the same sanatorium<br><br><br>To which you sent me? [[motifs/well\|Well]], he needs it more than I did. |
| REILLY | I am glad that you have come to see it in that light —<br><br><br>At least, for the moment. But, Mrs. Chamberlayne,<br>You have never visited my sanatorium. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | What do you mean? I asked to be sent<br><br><br>And you took me there. If that was not a sanatorium<br>What was it? |
| REILLY | A kind of hotel. A retreat<br><br><br>For people who imagine that they need a respite<br>From everyday life. They return refreshed;<br>And if they believe it to be a sanatorium<br>That is good reason for not sending them to one.<br>The people who need my sort of sanatorium<br>Are not easily deceived. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Are you a devil<br><br><br>Or merely a lunatic practical joker? |
| EDWARD | I incline to the second explanation<br><br><br>Without the qualification ‘lunatic’.<br>Why should you go to a sanatorium?<br><br><br>I have never known anyone in my life<br>With fewer mental complications than you;<br>You’re stronger than a … battleship. That’s what drove me mad.<br>I am the one who needs a sanatorium —<br>But I’m not going there. |
| REILLY | You are right, Mr. Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>You are no case for my sanatorium:<br>You are much too ill. |
| EDWARD | Much too ill?<br><br><br>Then I’ll go and be ill in a suburban boarding-[[house]]. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | That would never suit you, Edward. Now I know of a hotel<br><br><br>In the New Forest … |
| EDWARD | How like you, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]].<br><br><br>You always know of something better. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | It’s only that I have a more practical mind<br><br><br>Than you have, Edward. You do know that. |
| EDWARD | Only because you’ve told me so often.<br><br><br>I’d like to see you filling up an income-tax form. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Don’t be silly, Edward. When I say practical,<br><br><br>I mean practical in the things that really matter. |
| REILLY | May I interrupt this interesting discussion?<br><br><br>I say you are both too ill. There are several symptoms<br>Which must occur together, and to a marked degree,<br>To qualify a patient for my sanatorium:<br><br><br>And one of them is an honest mind.<br>That is one of the causes of their [[concepts/suffering\|suffering]]. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | No one can say my husband has an honest mind. |
| EDWARD | And I could not honestly say that of you, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]. |
| REILLY | I congratulate you both on your perspicacity.<br><br><br>Your sympathetic understanding of each other<br>Will prepare you to appreciate what I have to say to you.<br>I do not trouble myself with the common cheat,<br>Or with the insuperably, innocently dull:<br>My patients such as you are the [[concepts/self\|self]]-deceivers<br>Taking infinite pains, exhausting their energy,<br>Yet never quite successful. You have both of you pretended<br>To be consulting me; both, tried to impose upon me<br>Your own diagnosis, and prescribe your own cure.<br>But when you put yourselves into hands like mine<br>You surrender a great deal more than you meant to.<br>This is the consequence of trying to lie to me. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | I did not come here to be insulted. |
| REILLY | You have come where the word ‘insult’ has no meaning;<br><br><br>And you must put up with that. All that you have told me —<br>Both of you — was true enough: you described your feelings —<br>Or some of them — omitting the important facts.<br>Let me take your husband first.<br>[To EDWARD]                               You were lying to me<br><br><br>By concealing your relations with Miss Coplestone. |
| EDWARD | This is monstrous! My wife knew nothing about it. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Really, Edward! Even if I’d been blind<br><br><br>There were plenty of people to let me know about it.<br>I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] if there was anyone who didn’t know. |
| REILLY | There was one, in fact. But you, Mrs. Chamberlayne,<br><br><br>Tried to make me believe that it was this discovery<br>Precipitated what you called your nervous breakdown. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | But it’s true! I was completely prostrated;<br><br><br>Even if I have made a partial recovery. |
| REILLY | Certainly, you were completely prostrated,<br><br><br>And certainly, you have somewhat recovered.<br>But you failed to mention that the cause of your distress<br>Was the defection of your [[archetypes/lover\|lover]] — who suddenly<br>For the first [[motifs/time\|time]] in his life, fell in [[motifs/love\|love]] with someone,<br>And with someone of whom you had reason to be jealous. |
| EDWARD | Really, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]! This is very interesting.<br><br><br>You seem to have been much more successful at concealment<br>Than I was. Now I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] who it could have been. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], tell him if you like. |
| REILLY | A young man named Peter. |
| EDWARD | Peter? Peter who? |
| REILLY | Mr. Peter Quilpe<br><br><br>Was a frequent guest. |
| EDWARD | Peter Quilpe.<br><br><br>Peter Quilpe! Really [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]]!<br>I congratulate you. You could not have chosen<br>Anyone I was less likely to suspect.<br>And then he came to me to confide about [[characters/celia\|Celia]]!<br><br><br>I have never heard anything so utterly ludicrous:<br>This is the best joke that ever happened. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | I never knew you had such a sense of [[Humour\|humour]]. |
| REILLY | It is the first more hopeful symptom. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | How did you know all this? |
| REILLY | That I cannot disclose.<br><br><br>I have my own method of collecting information<br>About my patients. You must not ask me to reveal it —<br>That is a matter of professional etiquette. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | I have not noticed much professional etiquette<br><br><br>About your behaviour to-day. |
| REILLY | A point [[motifs/well\|well]] taken.<br><br><br>But permit me to remark that my revelations<br>About each of you, to one another,<br>Have not been of anything that you confided to me.<br>The information I have exchanged between you<br>Was all obtained from outside sources.<br>Mrs. Chamberlayne, when you came to me two months ago<br>I was dissatisfied with your explanation<br>Of your obvious symptoms of emotional strain<br>And so I made enquiries. |
| EDWARD | It was two months ago<br><br><br>That your breakdown began! and I never noticed it. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | You wouldn’t notice anything. You never noticed me. |
| REILLY | Now, I want to point out to both of you<br><br><br>How much you have in common. Indeed, I consider<br>That you are exceptionally [[motifs/well\|well]]-suited to each other.<br>Mr. Chamberlayne, when you thought your wife had left you,<br>You discovered, to your surprise and consternation,<br>That you were not really in [[motifs/love\|love]] with Miss Coplestone … |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | My husband has never been in [[motifs/love\|love]] with anybody. |
| REILLY | And were not prepared to make the least sacrifice<br><br><br>On her account. This injured your [[concepts/vanity\|vanity]].<br>You liked to think of yourself as a passionate [[archetypes/lover\|lover]].<br>Then you realised, what your wife has justly remarked,<br>That you had never been in [[motifs/love\|love]] with anybody;<br>Which made you suspect that you were incapable<br>Of loving. To men of a certain type<br>The suspicion that they are incapable of loving<br>Is as disturbing to their [[concepts/self\|self]]-esteem<br>As, in cruder men, the [[concepts/fear\|fear]] of impotence. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | You are cold-hearted, Edward. |
| REILLY | So you say, Mrs. Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>And now, let us turn to your side of the problem.<br>When you discovered that your young friend<br>(Though you knew, in your heart, that he was not in [[motifs/love\|love]] with you,<br><br><br>And were always humiliated by the awareness<br><br><br>That you had forced him into this position) —<br>When, I say, you discovered that your young friend<br>Had actually fallen in [[motifs/love\|love]] with Miss Coplestone,<br>It took you some [[motifs/time\|time]], I have no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]],<br>Before you would admit it. Though perhaps you knew it<br>Before he did. You pretended to yourself,<br>I suspect, and for as long as you could,<br>That he was aiming at a higher social distinction<br>Than the [[concepts/honour\|honour]] conferred by being your [[archetypes/lover\|lover]].<br><br><br>When you had to face the fact that his feelings towards her<br>Were different from any you had aroused in him —<br>It was a shock. You had wanted to be loved;<br>You had come to see that no one had ever loved you.<br>Then you began to [[concepts/fear\|fear]] that no one could [[motifs/love\|love]] you. |
| EDWARD | I’m beginning to feel very sorry for you, [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]].<br><br><br>You know, you really are exceptionally unlovable,<br>And I never quite knew why. I thought it was my fault. |
| REILLY | And now you begin to see, I [[concepts/hope\|hope]],<br><br><br>How much you have in common. The same isolation.<br>A man who finds himself incapable of loving<br>And a woman who finds that no man can [[motifs/love\|love]] her. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | It seems to me that what we have in common<br><br><br>Might be just enough to make us loathe one another. |
| REILLY | See it rather as the bond which holds you together.<br><br><br>While still in a state of unenlightenment,<br>You could always say: ‘he could not [[motifs/love\|love]] any woman;’<br><br><br>You could always say: ‘no man could [[motifs/love\|love]] her.’<br><br><br>You could accuse each other of your own faults,<br>And so could avoid understanding each other.<br>Now, you have only to reverse the propositions<br>And put them together. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Is that possible? |
| REILLY | If I had sent either of you to the sanatorium<br><br><br>In the state in which you came to me — I tell you this:<br>It would have been a horror beyond your imagining,<br>For you would have been left with what you brought with you:<br>The [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]] of desires of desires. A prey<br>To the devils who arrive at their plenitude of power<br>When they have you to themselves. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Then what can we do<br><br><br>When we can go neither back nor forward? Edward!<br>What can we do? |
| REILLY | You have answered your own question,<br><br><br>Though you do not know the meaning of what you have said. |
| EDWARD | [[characters/lavinia\|Lavinia]], we must make the best of a bad job.<br><br><br>That is what he means. |
| REILLY | When you find, Mr. Chamberlayne,<br><br><br>The best of a bad job is all any of us make of it —<br>Except of course, the saints — such as those who go<br>To the sanatorium — you will forget this phrase,<br>And in forgetting it will alter the condition. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Edward, there is that hotel in the New Forest<br><br><br>If you want to go there. The proprietor<br>Who has just taken over, is a friend of Alex’s.<br>I could go down with you, and then leave you there<br>If you want to be [[Alone\|alone]] … |
| EDWARD | But I can’t go away!<br><br><br>I have a case coming on next Monday. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Then will you stop at your club? |
| EDWARD | No, they won’t let me.<br><br><br>I must leave tomorrow — but how did you know<br>I was staying at the club? |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Really, Edward!<br><br><br>I have some sense of responsibility.<br><br><br>I was going to leave some shirts there for you. |
| EDWARD | It seems to me that I might as [[motifs/well\|well]] go [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Then we can share a taxi, and be economical.<br><br><br>Edward, have you anything else to ask him<br>Before we go? |
| EDWARD | Yes, I have.<br><br><br>But it’s difficult to say. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | But I wish you would say it.<br><br><br>At least, there is something I would like you to ask. |
| EDWARD | It’s about the future of … the others.<br><br><br>I don’t want to build on other people’s ruins. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | Exactly. And I have a question too.<br><br><br>Sir Henry, was it you who sent those telegrams? |
| REILLY | I think I will dispose of your husband’s problem.<br><br><br>[To EDWARD] Your business is not to clear your [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]<br><br><br>But to learn how to bear the burdens on your [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]].<br>With the future of the others you are not concerned. |
| [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]] | I think you have answered my question too.<br><br><br>They had to tell us, themselves, that they had made their decision. |
| EDWARD | Have you anything else to say to us, Sir Henry? |
| REILLY | No. Not in this capacity. |
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD takes out his cheque-book. REILLY raises his hand] |
| REILLY | My secretary will send you my account.<br>Go in peace. And work out your salvation with diligence. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exeunt EDWARD and [[characters/lavinia\|LAVINIA]]] |
| *(didascalia)* | [REILLY goes to the couch and lies down. The [[house]]-telephone rings. He gets up and answers it.] |
| REILLY | Yes? … Yes. Come in. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] by side door] |
| REILLY |                                                        She’s waiting downstairs. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | I know that, Henry. I brought her here myself. |
| REILLY | Oh? You didn’t let her know you were seeing me first? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Of course not. I dropped her at the door<br><br><br>And went on in the taxi, round the corner;<br>Waited a moment, and slipped in by the back way.<br>I only came to tell you, I am sure she is ready<br>To make a decision. |
| REILLY | Was she reluctant?<br><br><br>Was that why you brought her? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh no, not reluctant:<br><br><br>Only diffident. She cannot believe<br>That you will take her seriously. |
| REILLY | That is not uncommon. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Or that she deserves to be taken seriously. |
| REILLY | That is most uncommon. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Henry, get up.<br><br><br>You can’t be as tired as that. I shall wait in the next room,<br>And come back when she’s gone. |
| REILLY | Yes, when she’s gone. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Will Alex be here? |
| REILLY | Yes, he’ll be here. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] by side door] |
| *(didascalia)* | [REILLY presses button. [[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY shows in [[characters/celia\|CELIA]]] |
| REILLY | Miss [[characters/celia-coplestone\|Celia Coplestone]]? … Won’t you sit down?<br><br><br>I believe you are a friend of Mrs. Shuttlethwaite. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Yes, it was [[characters/julia\|Julia]] … Mrs. Shuttlethwaite<br><br><br>Who advised me to come to you. — But I’ve met you before,<br>Haven’t I, somewhere? … Oh, of course.<br>But I didn’t know … |
| REILLY | There is nothing you need to know.<br><br><br>I was there at the instance of Mrs. Shuttlethwaite. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | That makes it even more perplexing. However,<br><br><br>I don’t want to waste your [[motifs/time\|time]]. And I’m awfully afraid<br>That you’ll think that I am wasting it anyway.<br>I suppose most people, when they come to see you,<br>Are obviously ill, or can give good reasons<br>For wanting to see you. [[motifs/well\|Well]], I can’t.<br>I just came in desperation. And I shan’t be offended<br>If you simply tell me to go away again. |
| REILLY | Most of my patients begin, Miss Coplestone,<br><br><br>By telling me exactly what is the matter with them,<br>And what I am to do about it. They are quite sure<br>They have had a nervous breakdown — that is what they call it —<br>And usually they think that someone else is to blame. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I at least have no one to blame but myself. |
| REILLY | And after that, the prologue to my treatment<br><br><br>Is to try to show them that they are mistaken<br>About the [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of their illness, and lead them to see<br>That it’s not so interesting as they had imagined.<br>When I get as far as that, there is something to be done. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I can’t pretend that my trouble is interesting;<br><br><br>But I shan’t begin that way. I feel perfectly [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>I could lead an active life — if there’s anything to work for;<br>I don’t imagine that I am being persecuted;<br>I don’t hear any voices, I have no delusions —<br>Except that the world I live in seems all a delusion!<br>But oughtn’t I first to tell you the circumstances?<br>I’d forgotten that you know nothing about me;<br>And with what I’ve been going through, these last weeks,<br>I somehow took it for granted that I needn’t explain myself. |
| REILLY | I know quite enough about you for the moment:<br><br><br>Try first to describe your present state of mind. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], there are two things I can’t understand,<br><br><br>Which you might consider symptoms. But first I must tell you<br>That I should really like to think there’s something wrong with me —<br><br><br>Because, if there isn’t, then there’s something wrong,<br>Or at least, very different from what it seemed to be,<br>With the world itself — and that’s much more frightening!<br>That would be terrible. So I’d rather believe<br>There is something wrong with me, that could be put right.<br>I’d do anything you told me, to get back to normality. |
| REILLY | We must find out about you, before we decide<br><br><br>What is normality. You say there are two things:<br><br><br>What is the first? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | An awareness of [[concepts/solitude\|solitude]].<br><br><br>But that sounds so flat. I don’t mean simply<br>That there’s been a crash: though indeed there has been.<br>It isn’t simply the end of an illusion<br>In the ordinary way, or being ditched.<br>Of course that’s something that’s always happening<br>To all sorts of people, and they get over it<br>More or less, or at least they carry on.<br>No. I mean that what has happened has made me aware<br>That I’ve always been [[Alone\|alone]]. That one always is [[Alone\|alone]].<br>Not simply the ending of one relationship,<br>Not even simply finding that it never existed —<br>But a revelation about my relationship<br>With everybody. Do you know —<br><br><br>It no longer seems worth while to speak to anyone! |
| REILLY | And what about your parents? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Oh, they live in the country,<br><br><br>Now they can’t afford to have a place in town.<br>It’s all they can do to keep the country [[house]] going:<br>But it’s been in the family so long, they won’t leave it. |
| REILLY | And you live in [[settings/london\|London]]? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I share a flat<br><br><br>With a cousin: but she’s abroad at the moment,<br>And my family want me to come down and stay with them.<br>But I just can’t face it. |
| REILLY | So you want to see no one? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | No … it isn’t that I want to be [[Alone\|alone]],<br><br><br>But that everyone’s [[Alone\|alone]] — or so it seems to me.<br>They make noises, and think they are talking to each other;<br>They make faces, and think they understand each other.<br>And I’m sure that they don’t. Is that a delusion? |
| REILLY | A delusion is something we must return from.<br><br><br>There are other states of mind, which we take to be delusion,<br>But which we have to accept and go on from.<br>And the second symptom? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | That’s stranger still.<br><br><br>It sounds ridiculous — but the only word for it<br>That I can find, is a sense of sin. |
| REILLY | You suffer from a sense of sin, Miss Coplestone?<br><br><br>This is most unusual. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | It seemed to me abnormal. |
| REILLY | We have yet to find what would be normal<br>For you, before we use the term ‘abnormal’.<br><br><br>Tell me what you mean by a sense of sin. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | It’s much easier to tell you what I don’t mean:<br><br><br>I don’t mean sin in the ordinary sense. |
| REILLY | And what, in your opinion, is the ordinary sense? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] … I suppose it’s being immoral —<br><br><br>And I don’t feel as if I was immoral:<br>In fact, aren’t the people one thinks of as immoral<br>Just the people who we say have no moral sense?<br>I’ve never noticed that immorality<br>Was accompanied by a sense of sin:<br>At least, I have never come across it.<br>I suppose it is wicked to hurt other people<br>If you know that you’re hurting them. I haven’t hurt her.<br>I wasn’t taking anything away from her —<br>Anything she wanted. I may have been a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]:<br>But I don’t mind at all having been a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. |
| REILLY | And what is the point of view of your family? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], my bringing up was pretty conventional —<br><br><br>I had always been taught to disbelieve in sin.<br>Oh, I don’t mean that it was ever mentioned!<br>But anything wrong, from our point of view,<br>Was either bad form, or was psychological.<br>And bad form always led to disaster<br>Because the people one knew disapproved of it.<br>I don’t worry much about form, myself —<br>But when everything’s bad form, or mental kinks,<br>You either become bad form, and cease to care,<br>Or else, if you care, you must be kinky. |
| REILLY | And so you suppose you have what you call a ‘kink’? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But everything seemed so right, at the [[motifs/time\|time]]!<br><br><br>I’ve been thinking about it, over and over;<br>I can see now, it was all a mistake:<br>But I don’t see why mistakes should make one feel sinful!<br>And yet I can’t find any other word for it.<br>It must be some kind of hallucination;<br>Yet, at the same [[motifs/time\|time]], I’m frightened by the [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>That it is more real than anything I believed in. |
| REILLY | What is more real than anything you believed in? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | It’s not the feeling of anything I’ve ever done,<br>Which I might get away from, or of anything in me<br>I could get rid of — but of emptiness, of failure<br>Towards someone, or something, outside of myself;<br>And I feel I must … atone — is that the word?<br><br><br>Can you treat a patient for such a state of mind? |
| REILLY | What had you believed were your relations with this man? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Oh, you’d guessed that, had you? That’s clever of you.<br><br><br>No, perhaps I made it obvious. You don’t need to know<br>About him, do you? |
| REILLY | No. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Perhaps I’m only typical. |
| REILLY | There are different types. Some are rarer than others. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Oh, I thought that I was giving him so much!<br><br><br>And he to me — and the giving and the taking<br>Seemed so right: not in terms of calculation<br>Of what was good for the persons we had been<br>But for the new person, us. If I could feel<br><br><br>As I did then, even now it would seem right.<br>And then I found we were only strangers<br>And that there had been neither giving nor taking<br>But that we had merely made use of each other<br>Each for his purpose. That’s horrible. Can we only [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Something created by our own [[concepts/imagination\|imagination]]?<br>Are we all in fact unloving and unlovable?<br>Then one is [[Alone\|alone]], and if one is [[Alone\|alone]]<br><br><br>Then [[archetypes/lover\|lover]] and beloved are equally unreal<br>And the dreamer is no more real than his [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]]. |
| REILLY | And this man. What does he now seem like, to you? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Like a [[archetypes/child\|child]] who has wandered into a forest<br><br><br>Playing with an imaginary playmate<br>And suddenly discovers he is only a [[archetypes/child\|child]]<br>Lost in a forest, wanting to go [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| REILLY | Compassion may be already a clue<br><br><br>Towards finding your own way out of the forest. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But even if I find my way out of the forest<br><br><br>I shall be left with the inconsolable [[motifs/memory\|memory]]<br>Of the treasure I went into the forest to find<br>And never found, and which was not there<br>And perhaps is not anywhere? But if not anywhere,<br>Why do I feel guilty at not having found it? |
| REILLY | Disillusion can become itself an illusion<br><br><br>If we rest in it. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I cannot argue.<br><br><br>It’s not that I’m afraid of being hurt again:<br>Nothing again can either hurt or heal.<br>I have thought at moments that the ecstasy is real<br>Although those who experience it may have no reality.<br>For what happened is remembered like [[A Dream\|a dream]]<br>In which one is exalted by intensity of loving<br>In the spirit, a vibration of delight<br>Without [[motifs/desire\|desire]], for [[motifs/desire\|desire]] is fulfilled<br>In the delight of loving. A state one does not know<br>When awake. But what, or whom I loved,<br>Or what in me was loving, I do not know.<br>And if that is all meaningless, I want to be cured<br>Of a craving for something I cannot find<br>And of the shame of never finding it.<br>Can you cure me? |
| REILLY | The condition is curable.<br><br><br>But the form of treatment must be your own choice:<br>I cannot choose for you. If that is what you wish,<br>I can reconcile you to the human condition,<br>The condition to which some who have gone as far as you<br>Have succeeded in returning. They may remember<br>The vision they have had, but they cease to regret it,<br>Maintain themselves by the common routine,<br>Learn to avoid excessive expectation,<br>Become tolerant of themselves and others,<br>Giving and taking, in the usual actions<br>What there is to give and take. They do not repine;<br>Are contented with the morning that separates<br>And with the evening that brings together<br>For casual talk before the [[motifs/fire\|fire]]<br>Two people who know they do not understand each other,<br>Breeding children whom they do not understand<br>And who will never understand them. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Is that the best life? |
| REILLY | It is a good life. Though you will not know how good<br><br><br>Till you come to the end. But you will want nothing else,<br>And the other life will be only like a book<br>You have read once, and lost. In a world of lunacy,<br>Violence, stupidity, [[concepts/greed\|greed]] … it is a good life. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I know I ought to be able to accept that<br><br><br>If I might still have it. Yet it [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] me cold.<br>Perhaps that’s just a part of my illness,<br>But I feel it would be a kind of surrender —<br>No, not a surrender — more like a betrayal.<br>You see, I think I really had a vision of something<br>Though I don’t know what it is. I don’t want to forget it.<br>I want to live with it. I could do without everything,<br>Put up with anything, if I might cherish it.<br>In fact, I think it would really be dishonest<br>For me, now, to try to make a life with anybody!<br><br><br>I couldn’t give anyone the kind of [[motifs/love\|love]] —<br>I wish I could — which belongs to that life.<br>Oh, I’m afraid this sounds like raving!<br>Or just cantankerousness … still,<br>If there’s no other way … then I feel just hopeless. |
| REILLY | There is another way, if you have the [[concepts/courage\|courage]].<br><br><br>The first I could describe in familiar terms<br>Because you have seen it, as we all have seen it,<br>Illustrated, more or less, in lives of those about us.<br>The second is unknown, and so requires [[concepts/faith\|faith]] —<br>The kind of [[concepts/faith\|faith]] that issues from [[concepts/despair\|despair]].<br>The destination cannot be described;<br>You will know very little until you get there;<br>You will [[motifs/journey\|journey]] blind. But the way leads towards possession<br>Of what you have sought for in the wrong place. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | That sounds like what I want. But what is my duty? |
| REILLY | Whichever way you choose will prescribe its own duty. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Which way is better? |
| REILLY | Neither way is better.<br><br><br>Both ways are necessary. It is also necessary<br>To make a choice between them. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Then I choose the second. |
| REILLY | It is a terrifying [[motifs/journey\|journey]]. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I am not frightened<br><br><br>But glad. I suppose it is a lonely way? |
| REILLY | No lonelier than the other. But those who take the other<br>Can forget their [[concepts/loneliness\|loneliness]]. You will not forget yours.<br>Each way means [[concepts/loneliness\|loneliness]] — and communion.<br>Both ways avoid the final desolation<br>Of [[concepts/solitude\|solitude]] in the phantasmal world<br>Of [[concepts/imagination\|imagination]], shuffling memories and desires. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | That is the hell I have been in. |
| REILLY | It isn’t hell<br><br><br>Till you become incapable of anything else.<br>Now — do you feel quite sure? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I want your second way.<br><br><br>So what am I to do? |
| REILLY | You will go to the sanatorium. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Oh, what an anti-climax! I have known people<br><br><br>Who have been to your sanatorium, and come back again —<br>I don’t mean to say they weren’t much better for it —<br>That’s why I came to you. But they returned …<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] … I mean … to everyday life. |
| REILLY | True. But the friends you have in mind<br><br><br>Cannot have been to this sanatorium.<br>I am very careful whom I send there:<br>Those who go do not come back as these did. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | It sounds like a [[motifs/prison\|prison]]. But they can’t all stay there!<br><br><br>I mean, it would make the place so over-crowded. |
| REILLY | Not very many go. But I said they did not come back<br><br><br>In the sense in which your friends came back.<br>I did not say they stayed there. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | What becomes of them? |
| REILLY | They choose, Miss Coplestone. Nothing is forced on them.<br><br><br>Some of them return, in a physical sense;<br>No one disappears. They lead very active lives<br>Very often, in the world. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | How soon will you send me there? |
| REILLY | How soon will you be ready? |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | Tonight, by nine o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]]. |
| REILLY | Go [[motifs/home\|home]] then, and make your preparations.<br><br><br>Here is the address for you to give your friends; |
| *(didascalia)* | [Writes on a slip of paper] |
| REILLY | You had better let your family know at once.<br>I will send a car for you at nine o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]]. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | What do I need to take with me? |
| REILLY | Nothing.<br><br><br>Everything you need will be provided for you,<br>And you will have no expenses at the sanatorium. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | I don’t in the least know what I am doing<br><br><br>Or why I am doing it. There is nothing else to do:<br>That is the only reason. |
| REILLY | It is the best reason. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But I know it is I who have made the decision:<br><br><br>I must tell you that. Oh, I almost forgot —<br>May I ask what your fee is? |
| REILLY | I have told my secretary<br><br><br>That there is no fee. |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | But … |
| REILLY | For a case like yours<br><br><br>There is no fee. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Presses button] |
| [[characters/celia\|CELIA]] | You have been very kind. |
| REILLY | Go in peace, my daughter.<br><br><br>Work out your salvation with diligence. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY appears at door. Exit [[characters/celia\|CELIA]]. REILLY dials on [[house]]-telephone.] |
| REILLY | REILLY [into telephone]. It is finished. You can come in now. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] by side door] |
| REILLY | She will go far, that one. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Very far, I think.<br><br><br>You do not need to tell me. I knew from the beginning. |
| REILLY | It’s the other ones I am worried about. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Nonsense, Henry, I shall keep an eye on them. |
| REILLY | To send them back: what have they to go back tc?<br><br><br>To the stale food mouldering in the larder,<br>The stale thoughts mouldering in their minds.<br>Each unable to disguise his own meanness<br>From himself, because it is known to the other.<br>It’s not the knowledge of the mutual treachery<br>But the knowledge that the other understands the motive —<br>[[motifs/mirror\|Mirror]] to [[motifs/mirror\|mirror]], reflecting [[concepts/vanity\|vanity]].<br>I have taken a great risk. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | We must always take risks.<br><br><br>That is our destiny. Since you question the decision<br>What possible alternative can you imagine? |
| REILLY | None. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Very [[motifs/well\|well]] then. We must take the risk.<br><br><br>All we could do was to give them the chance.<br>And now, when they are stripped naked to their souls<br>And can choose, whether to put on proper costumes<br>Or huddle quickly into new disguises,<br>They have, for the first [[motifs/time\|time]], somewhere to start from.<br>Oh, of course, they might just murder each other!<br>But I don’t think they will do that. We shall see.<br>It’s the thought of [[characters/celia\|Celia]] that weighs upon my mind. |
| REILLY | Of [[characters/celia\|Celia]]? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Of [[characters/celia\|Celia]]. |
| REILLY | But when I said just now<br><br><br>That she would go far, you agreed with me. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Oh yes, she will go far. And we know where she is going.<br><br><br>But what do we know of the terrors of the [[motifs/journey\|journey]]?<br>You and I don’t know the process by which the human is<br>Transhumanised: what do we know<br>Of the kind of [[concepts/suffering\|suffering]] they must undergo<br>On the way of illumination? |
| REILLY | Will she be frightened<br><br><br>By the first appearance of projected spirits? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Henry, you simply do not understand [[innocence]].<br><br><br>She will be afraid of nothing; she will not even know<br>That there is anything there to be afraid of.<br>She is too humble. She will pass between the scolding hills,<br>Through the valley of derision, like a [[archetypes/child\|child]] sent on an errand<br>In eagerness and patience. Yet she must suffer. |
| REILLY | When I express confidence in anything<br><br><br>You always raise doubts; when I am apprehensive<br>Then you see no reason for anything but confidence. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | That’s one way in which I am so useful to you.<br><br><br>You ought to be grateful. |
| REILLY | And when I say to one like her<br><br><br>‘Work out your salvation with diligence’, I do not understand<br>What I myself am saying. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | You must accept your limitations.<br><br><br>— But how much longer will Alex keep us waiting? |
| REILLY | He should be here by now. I’ll speak to Miss Barraway. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Takes up [[house]]-telephone] |
| REILLY | Miss Barraway, when Mr. Gibbs arrive …<br>Oh, very good.<br>[To [[characters/julia\|JULIA]]] He’s on his way up. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Into telephone] |
| REILLY | You may bring the tray in now, Miss Barraway. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter ALEX] |
| ALEX | [[motifs/well\|Well]]! [[motifs/well\|Well]]! and how have we got on? |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Everything is in order. |
| ALEX | The Chamberlaynes have chosen? |
| REILLY | They accept their destiny. |
| ALEX | And she has made the choice? |
| REILLY | She will be fetched this evening. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[[characters/nurse\|NURSE]]-SECRETARY enters with a tray, a decanter and three glasses, and exit. REILLY pours drinks.] |
| REILLY | And now we are ready to proceed to the libation. |
| ALEX | The words for the building of the hearth. |
| *(didascalia)* | [They raise their glasses] |
| REILLY | Let them build the hearth<br><br><br>Under the protection of the [[motifs/stars\|stars]]. |
| ALEX | Let them place a chair each side of it. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | May the holy ones watch over the roof,<br><br><br>May the [[motifs/moon\|moon]] herself influence the bed. |
| *(didascalia)* | [They drink] |
| ALEX | The words for those who go upon a [[motifs/journey\|journey]]. |
| REILLY | Protector of travellers<br><br><br>Bless the [[motifs/road\|road]]. |
| ALEX | Watch over her in the [[motifs/desert\|desert]].<br><br><br>Watch over her in the mountain.<br>Watch over her in the labyrinth.<br>Watch over her by the quicksand. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Protect her from the Voices<br>Protect her from the Visions<br>Protect her in the tumult<br>Protect her in the [[motifs/silence\|silence]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [They drink] |
| REILLY | There is one for whom the words cannot be spoken. |
| ALEX | They can not be spoken yet. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | You mean Peter Quilpe. |
| REILLY | He has not yet come to where the words are valid. |
| [[characters/julia\|JULIA]] | Shall we ever speak them? |
| ALEX | Others, perhaps, will speak them.<br><br><br>You know, I have connections — even in California.<br>CURTAIN |

| Chi parla | Battuta |  
|---|---|  
| *(didascalia)* | Lo studio di [[characters/sir-henry-harcourt-reilly\|SIR HENRY HARCOURT-REILLY]] a [[settings/london\|Londra]]. Mattino: diverse settimane dopo. SIR HENRY [[Alone\|solo]] alla sua scrivania. Preme un pulsante elettrico. Entra l’[[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA, con l’agenda degli appuntamenti. |  
| REILLY | Riguardo a quei tre appuntamenti di stamattina, signorina Barraway:<br><br><br>vorrei ripassare ancora una volta le mie istruzioni.<br>Capisce, naturalmente, che è importante<br>evitare qualsiasi incontro? |  
| [[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA | L’ha detto chiaramente, Sir Henry:<br><br><br>il primo appuntamento alle undici in [[motifs/clock\|punto]].<br>Va fatto accomodare nella saletta d’attesa;<br>e lei lo riceverà quasi subito. |  
| REILLY | Lo riceverò subito. E il secondo? |  
| [[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA | La seconda va fatta accomodare nell’altra stanza<br><br><br>come al solito. Arriva alle undici e un quarto;<br>ma lei può farla attendere. |  
| REILLY | O può darsi che sia lei a far attendere me;<br><br><br>ma credo che sarà puntuale. |  
| [[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA | Le telefono<br><br><br>nell’istante in cui arriva. La lascio lì<br>finché lei non suona tre volte. |  
| REILLY | E la terza paziente? |  
| [[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA | La terza va fatta accomodare nella stanza piccola;<br><br><br>e non devo avvertirla che è arrivata.<br>Poi, quando lei suona, accompagno fuori gli altri;<br>e solo dopo che avranno lasciato la [[house\|casa]]…. |  
| REILLY | Esatto, signorina Barraway. Per ora è tutto. |  
| [[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA | C’è il signor Gibbs, Sir Henry. |  
| REILLY | Lo faccia entrare subito. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Esce l’[[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA] |  
| *(didascalia)* | [ALEX entra quasi subito] |  
| ALEX | Quando è l’appuntamento di Chamberlayne? |  
| REILLY | Alle undici in [[motifs/clock\|punto]],<br><br><br>l’ora convenzionale. Non abbiamo molto [[motifs/time\|tempo]].<br>Dimmi ora, hai avuto qualche difficoltà<br>a convincerlo che ero io l’uomo per il suo caso? |  
| ALEX | Difficoltà? No! Era solo impaziente<br><br><br>di dover aspettare quattro giorni per l’appuntamento. |  
| REILLY | Era necessario rinviare il suo appuntamento<br><br><br>per abbassargli le resistenze. Ma quel che intendo è:<br>si fida del tuo giudizio? |  
| ALEX | Sì, ciecamente.<br><br><br>Non che mi consideri molto intelligente,<br>ma pensa che io sia [[motifs/well\|bene]] informato: il tipo di persona<br>che conosce il medico giusto, [[motifs/well\|come]] i negozi giusti.<br><br><br>Del resto, era pronto a consultare qualunque medico<br>raccomandato da chiunque tranne che da sua moglie. |  
| REILLY | Le avevo già fatto capire<br><br><br>che non doveva fargli il mio nome. |  
| ALEX | Con la tua solita preveggenza. Ora è tutto trionfante<br><br><br>perché crede di averla battuta sul tempo.<br>E quando l’avrai mandato in un sanatorio<br>dove lei non possa raggiungerlo — allora, è convinto,<br>lei sarà molto pentita. Si gode la sua malattia. |  
| REILLY | La malattia gli offre un [[topoi/double\|doppio]] vantaggio:<br><br><br>fuggire da sé stesso — e aver la meglio sulla moglie. |  
| ALEX | Non fuggire da lei? |  
| REILLY | Non vuole fuggire da lei. |  
| ALEX | Alloggia al suo club. |  
| REILLY | Sì, è di là che ha scritto. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Squilla il telefono di [[house\|casa]]] |  
| REILLY | Pronto! Sì, lo faccia salire. |  
| ALEX | Avrai una mattinata intensa!<br><br><br>Esco dalla scala di servizio<br>e torno quando se ne saranno andati. |  
| REILLY | Sì, quando se ne saranno andati. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Esce ALEX dalla porta laterale] |  
| *(didascalia)* | [EDWARD viene fatto entrare dall’[[characters/nurse\|INFERMIERA]]-SEGRETARIA] |  
| EDWARD | [[characters/sir-henry-harcourt-reilly\|Sir Henry Harcourt-Reilly]] — |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Si ferma e fissa REILLY] |  
| EDWARD | REILLY [senza alzare gli occhi dalle sue carte]. Buongiorno, signor Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>Prego, si accomodi. Non la tratterrò un istante.<br>— Dunque, signor Chamberlayne? |  
| EDWARD | Mi era passato per la mente,<br><br><br>prima di varcare la porta, che lei potesse essere la stessa persona:<br>ma l’ho scacciato come l’ennesimo sintomo.<br>[[motifs/well\|Beh]], avrei dovuto guardarmi dal venire qui<br>su raccomandazione di un uomo che non la conosceva.<br>Eppure Alex è così persuasivo. E i suoi consigli<br>sui negozi sono sempre stati soddisfacenti.<br>Le chiedo scusa. Ma è uno sbadato.<br><br><br>Vorrei sapere… ma a che serve!<br>Suppongo che [[motifs/well\|tanto varrebbe]] andarmene subito. |  
| REILLY | No. La prego, si sieda, signor Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>Non se ne andrà, dunque [[motifs/well\|tanto vale]] che si sieda.<br>Stava per fare una domanda. |  
| EDWARD | Quando venne nel mio appartamento<br><br><br>era stato invitato da mia moglie come ospite,<br>come supponevo? … O fu lei a mandarla? |  
| REILLY | Non posso dire di essere stato invitato;<br><br><br>e la signora Chamberlayne non sapeva che sarei venuto.<br>Ma sapevo che lei sarebbe stato lì, e chi avrei trovato con lei. |  
| EDWARD | Ma aveva visto mia moglie? |  
| REILLY | Oh sì, l’avevo vista. |  
| EDWARD | Allora questa è una trappola! |  
| REILLY | Non chiamiamola trappola.<br><br><br>Ma se è una trappola, allora non può sfuggirle:<br>e dunque… [[motifs/well\|tanto vale]] che si sieda.<br>Credo che troverà comoda quella poltrona. |  
| EDWARD | Lei sapeva,<br><br><br>prima che cominciassi a raccontarle, che cosa era accaduto? |  
| REILLY | È così, è così. Ma ogni cosa a suo [[motifs/time\|tempo]].<br><br><br>Lasciamo da parte quella domanda per ora.<br>Mi parli prima delle difficoltà<br>sulle quali desidera il mio parere professionale. |  
| EDWARD | Non sta a me rimproverarle di aver riportato indietro mia moglie,<br><br><br>suppongo. Sembrava che cercasse di persuadermi<br>che stavo meglio senza di lei. Ma non si rendeva conto<br>che non ero in condizione di prendere una decisione? |  
| REILLY | Se non avessi riportato indietro sua moglie, signor Chamberlayne,<br><br><br>crede che le cose andrebbero meglio — adesso? |  
| EDWARD | Non so, davvero. Difficilmente potrebbero andar peggio. |  
| REILLY | Potrebbero andar molto peggio. Poteva rovinare tre vite<br><br><br>con la sua indecisione. Ora sono soltanto due —<br>e ha ancora la possibilità di riscattarle dalla rovina. |  
| EDWARD | Lei parla come se io fossi capace di agire:<br><br><br>se lo fossi, non avrei bisogno di consultare lei<br>né nessun altro. Sono venuto qui come paziente.<br>Se il mio caso non le interessa, posso andare altrove. |  
| REILLY | Ha motivo di credere di essere molto malato? |  
| EDWARD | Avrei pensato che un medico potesse vederlo da sé.<br><br><br>O almeno che si informasse sui sintomi.<br>Due persone mi hanno consigliato di recente,<br>quasi con le stesse parole, di vedere un medico.<br>Hanno detto — di nuovo, quasi con le stesse parole —<br>che ero sull’orlo di un esaurimento nervoso.<br>Allora io stesso non lo sapevo — ma se loro lo vedevano<br>avrei pensato che un medico potesse vederlo. |  
| REILLY | «Esaurimento nervoso» è un termine che non uso mai:<br><br><br>può significare quasi tutto. |  
| EDWARD | E da allora mi sono reso conto<br><br><br>che il mio è un caso davvero insolito. |  
| REILLY | Ogni caso è unico, e assai simile agli altri. |  
| EDWARD | Esiste un sanatorio dove lei manda pazienti<br><br><br>come me, sotto la sua osservazione personale? |  
| REILLY | Lei è molto impetuoso, signor Chamberlayne.<br><br><br>Esistono diverse specie di sanatori<br>per diverse specie di pazienti. E vi sono anche pazienti<br>per i quali un sanatorio è il luogo peggiore possibile.<br>Dobbiamo prima scoprire che cosa non va in lei<br>prima di decidere che cosa fare di lei. |  
| EDWARD | [[concepts/doubt\|Dubito]] che lei abbia mai avuto un caso come il mio:<br><br><br>ho cessato di credere alla mia stessa personalità. |  
| REILLY | Oh, povero me; è grave. Un male comunissimo.<br><br><br>Diffusissimo, davvero. |  
| EDWARD | Ricordo, nella mia [[motifs/childhood\|infanzia]]… |  
| REILLY | Comincio sempre dalla situazione immediata<br><br><br>e poi risalgo indietro quanto trovo necessario.<br>Vede, i suoi ricordi d’[[motifs/childhood\|infanzia]] —<br>intendo, nel suo stato d’animo attuale —<br>sarebbero in gran parte fittizi; e quanto ai suoi [[motifs/dreams\|sogni]],<br>lei produrrebbe [[motifs/dreams\|sogni]] stupefacenti, per compiacermi.<br>Potrei farle sognare qualunque sogno le suggerissi,<br>e non servirebbe che a lusingare la sua [[concepts/vanity\|vanità]]<br>col temporaneo stimolo di sentirsi interessante. |  
| EDWARD | Ma io sono ossessionato dal pensiero della mia insignificanza. |  
| REILLY | Precisamente.
